  - 10 new event types registered at v1 per [D7] (place/asset/account × added/updated, money_flow × 3, embedding.generated).
  - 38 new unit tests (places_assets_accounts 15, money 13, vector_search 10) + integration rebuild extended to 10 projections + ~1200 events.

- **Carry-forward for future embedding daemon**:

### Prompt 07c-α — xlsx round-trip foundations (schema + sidecar + diff core)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Claude Chat, pre-session. Prompt file: prompts/07c-alpha-foundations.md.
- **Session merged**: PR #20, commits aa395dd / 7305acd / fcdb592 / 1d770ec, merged 2026-04-24.
- **Outcome**: MERGED
- **Note**: Part 1 of a two-prompt split. 07c-β consumes what this prompt lands and ships the reverse daemon + integration round-trip. Split exists per partner_handoff PM-2 / PM-15: full reverse daemon plus its test pyramid does not fit one Claude Code session window.
- **Evidence**:
  - Two new system events at v1 in `adminme/events/schemas/system.py`: `xlsx.reverse_projected` (workbook_name, detected_at, sheets_affected, events_emitted, duration_ms) and `xlsx.reverse_skipped_during_forward` (workbook_name, detected_at, skip_reason: forward_lock_held). Schema-registry test asserts both register at v1.
  - `scripts/verify_invariants.sh` `ALLOWED_EMITS` regex extended; `ALLOWED_EMIT_FILES` left alone with maintenance comment explaining why (07c-β's reverse daemon lives outside `adminme/projections/` so the projection-emit auditor doesn't apply).
  - PM-10 disposition: deleted `adminme/projections/xlsx_workbooks/forward.py`, `reverse.py`, `schemas.py` — three 22-line scaffolding stubs from prompt 02 with no callers. Pre-delete grep confirmed zero imports.
  - `InstanceConfig.xlsx_workbooks_dir` flipped from `projections/.xlsx-state/` to `projections/xlsx_workbooks/`. Sibling `.xlsx-state/` resolves via `xlsx_workbooks_dir.parent / ".xlsx-state"`. Sibling pathway is required so 07c-β's watchdog scoped to the workbooks dir cannot self-trigger on sidecar writes. Existing 29-test xlsx suite unchanged — all callers use `config.xlsx_workbooks_dir` directly so the path change is transparent.
  - Sidecar I/O module at `adminme/projections/xlsx_workbooks/sidecar.py`: `sidecar_dir`, `sidecar_path`, `write_sheet_state`, `write_readonly_state`, `read_sheet_state`, `read_readonly_state`, `hash_readonly_sheet`. Atomic writes via `.tmp.<pid>` + `os.replace`. Bidirectional sheets persist `{"rows": [...]}`; read-only sheets persist `{"content_hash": "<sha256-hex>"}` only (no row data — hash-only is sufficient for the WARN signal 07c-β needs on read-only edits).
  - Forward daemon extended: `XlsxWorkbooksProjection._regenerate` now calls a new `_write_sidecar_for(workbook, xlsx_path)` helper as the LAST step inside the workbook lock. Helper opens the just-written xlsx with `openpyxl.load_workbook(data_only=True)` and writes per-sheet sidecar JSON. Reading back from the xlsx (rather than re-querying projections) ensures byte-alignment between sidecar and workbook on disk.
  - Two module-level constants `_BIDIRECTIONAL_SHEETS` and `_READONLY_SHEETS` are now the single source of truth for which sheets each workbook contains; both the `xlsx.regenerated` payload's `sheets_regenerated` list and the sidecar writer derive from them.
  - New `adminme/daemons/xlsx_sync/` package with binding placement note: "this daemon is L1-adjacent, NOT a projection per [§2.2] — it emits events." Per-sheet diff descriptors at `sheet_schemas.py` (Tasks/Commitments/Recurrences/Raw Data) declaring id_column, editable_columns (frozenset OR per-row callable for Raw Data), always_derived, add/update/delete event mappings, undo-window flag, new_id_prefix, and drop dispositions for non-emitting cases. Pure-functional diff core at `diff.py` with type normalization (float tol 1e-9, datetime/date → isoformat, None ≡ "", int↔float as floats) and id-column-edit-as-delete-plus-add semantics. Diff core has zero I/O — no openpyxl, no watchdog, no event log.
  - 28 new unit tests across four sites: 1 schema-registry extension, 8 sidecar I/O, 4 forward-writes-sidecar, 15 diff core (the diff site exceeds the ≥10 requirement; sidecar site exceeds ≥6). [QC correction: PR description and earlier draft of this entry said "23 new" — actual tally is 1+8+4+15 = 28.]
  - Ruff clean, mypy clean, `bash scripts/verify_invariants.sh` clean. Full test suite passes.
  - **QC note (descriptor visibility):** descriptor symbols in `sheet_schemas.py` are private (`_TASKS`, `_COMMITMENTS`, `_RECURRENCES`, `_RAW_DATA`); accessible only via `descriptor_for(workbook, sheet)` and `editable_columns_for(descriptor, row)` and the `BIDIRECTIONAL_DESCRIPTORS` tuple. Earlier 07c-β draft cited them by `TASKS_DESCRIPTOR`-style names that don't exist; 07c-β was patched in refactor to use the public accessor everywhere. See PM-16.
  - **QC note (read_only flag):** `_write_sidecar_for` opens the just-written xlsx with `read_only=False`. `read_only=True` would be cheaper for read-back. Not a violation; not blocking; flagging for awareness only.
  - **QC note (test fixture amendment):** during Commit 3 development, the `money_flow.manually_added` test fixture initially omitted `added_by_party_id` and used a `linked_account` field since removed from schema. Claude Code amended the fixture to include `added_by_party_id: "p1"` and re-ran; tests passed. Healthy debug loop. Surfaces UT-7 (actor attribution) as relevant for 07c-β's daemon emit path — daemon will use `actor_identity="xlsx_reverse"` per 07c-β draft until prompt 08 closes UT-7.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 07c-β (reverse daemon + integration round-trip)**:
  - Descriptors at `adminme/daemons/xlsx_sync/sheet_schemas.py` — bidirectional set is exactly Tasks / Commitments / Recurrences / Raw Data. People / Accounts / Metadata are read-only; 07c-β handles them via a separate "WARN if hash drifted" code path, NOT via descriptors here. **Reach descriptors via `descriptor_for(workbook, sheet)`; iterate via `BIDIRECTIONAL_DESCRIPTORS`. Private symbols (`_TASKS` etc.) are not exported.**
  - Diff core at `adminme/daemons/xlsx_sync/diff.py` — sync, pure-functional, returns `DiffResult(added, updated, deleted, dropped_edits)`. Daemon wraps it with watchdog → asyncio bridge, lock acquisition, undo window, sensitivity preservation, cold-start handling.
  - Sidecar I/O at `adminme/projections/xlsx_workbooks/sidecar.py` — forward already writes inside its lock. Reverse must rewrite at the end of each cycle (per BUILD.md §3.11 line 1015) so two principal saves diff against each other. Path: `<instance_dir>/projections/.xlsx-state/<workbook>/<sheet>.json`, sibling of workbooks dir.
  - The reverse daemon emits domain events on principal authority. The full UT-7 actor-attribution path (which member id authored the edit) does NOT resolve here; it resolves in prompt 08 when the event router knows about Session/principal_member_id. 07c-β stubs actor with `actor_identity="xlsx_reverse"` per the draft's envelope template.
  - The `xlsx.regenerated` system event provides a signal 07c-β's reverse can use to skip diffing a workbook the forward daemon just wrote.
  - **Recurrences sheet shape:** the 07b-built Recurrences sheet has columns `recurrence_id, title, cadence, next_due, assigned_member, notes, active, last_completed_at`. There is no `rrule` column on the sheet — the projection's underlying `rrule` is rendered into the human-readable `cadence` column at forward time. 07c-β's Recurrences ADD must map sheet `cadence` → `RecurrenceAddedV1.rrule` directly. The schema accepts free-text (`str`, `min_length=1`) — no RFC 5545 validation. Whatever the principal typed in `cadence` passes through unchanged. (Earlier 07c-β draft assumed both columns existed; patched in refactor.)
- **Carry-forward for prompt 08 (Session + scope + governance)**:
  - UT-7 opens here, resolves there: route reverse-emitted events through Session/guardedWrite so the actor (`added_by_party_id`, `principal_member_id`, etc.) is principal-attributed.

### Prompt 07c-β — xlsx reverse daemon + integration round-trip
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Claude Chat, pre-session. Prompt file: prompts/07c-beta-reverse-daemon.md.
- **Session merged**: PR #21, commits ffa6d9c / bf649ed / 00bff7d / 2788761, merged 2026-04-25.
- **Outcome**: MERGED
- **Note**: Part 2 of a two-prompt split. 07c-α (PR #20, merged 2026-04-24) landed schema/descriptors/diff/sidecar; together with this prompt it closes the xlsx round-trip and resolves UT-6.
- **Evidence**:
  - `XlsxReverseDaemon` lands at `adminme/daemons/xlsx_sync/reverse.py`. Public API: `start()` (begins watchdog observer; idempotent), `stop()` (cancels pending cycles + undo-window tasks + observer; idempotent), `run_cycle_now(workbook)` (bypasses the watchdog and flush wait; used by tests). Constructor signature: `(config, query_context, *, event_log, flush_wait_s=2.0, forward_lock_timeout_s=10.0, delete_undo_window_s=5.0)` per spec.
  - Per-cycle algorithm follows BUILD.md §3.11 lines 993–1080 verbatim: flush wait → forward lock acquisition (timeout → emit `xlsx.reverse_skipped_during_forward` and return — NO `xlsx.reverse_projected`) → load workbook with `openpyxl.load_workbook(data_only=True)` → for each bidirectional sheet, diff live rows vs sidecar (cold-start sheets emit nothing) → for each read-only sheet, hash live rows + WARN on drift → rewrite ALL sidecar JSON to current state (so subsequent cycles diff cleanly) → release lock → emit `xlsx.reverse_projected` with `events_emitted` (envelope ids) + `sheets_affected` + `duration_ms`.
  - Watchdog→asyncio bridge: `watchdog.observers.Observer` schedules a `FileSystemEventHandler` whose callbacks run on watchdog's thread; the daemon hops to its asyncio loop via `loop.call_soon_threadsafe(self._schedule_cycle, workbook_name)`. The on-loop `_schedule_cycle` cancels any pending debounce for the workbook and schedules a fresh one after `flush_wait_s`. Per-workbook serialization is enforced by an internal `asyncio.Lock` keyed per workbook; tests assert `_max_concurrent_observed <= 1`.
  - Undo window: deletes on sheets where `descriptor.deletes_use_undo_window=True` queue an `asyncio.Task` via `_schedule_undo_delete` that sleeps `delete_undo_window_s` then emits the delete event. Subsequent cycles observing the row return cancel the task via `cancel_pending_delete`. Note: emits scheduled inside the undo window do NOT count toward the cycle's `events_emitted` list (they fire after the cycle's terminal `xlsx.reverse_projected`). Acceptable observability gap, documented in the daemon's module docstring.
  - All four bidirectional sheet pathways wired: Tasks (3 emit functions) + Commitments (3) + Recurrences (3) + Raw Data (2 — money_flow.manually_added + money_flow.manually_deleted; updates not allowed per descriptor). Each emit derives `actor_identity="xlsx_reverse"` (UT-7 placeholder).
  - Sensitivity preservation: every emit copies sensitivity from the sidecar's prior state (or defaults `normal` for ADDs). Privileged → privileged; sensitive → sensitive.
  - 22 new tests: 6 unit (schema registry, descriptor bridge, daemon shape, undo-window cancel, sidecar rewrite at end of cycle, lock-contention skip path) + 6 integration (round-trip per pathway, plus cold-start, multi-sheet, read-only WARN drift) + 10 algorithm-cell coverage (each emit path + the lock-contention emit + the WARN emit).
  - `scripts/demo_xlsx_roundtrip.py` smoke script demonstrates user edit → cycle → emit → rebuild → workbook restored.
  - Ruff clean, mypy clean, `bash scripts/verify_invariants.sh` clean. Full test suite 411 passed / 1 skipped.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 08 (Session + scope + governance — opens UT-7)**:
  - Reverse daemon emits use `actor_identity="xlsx_reverse"` literal at line 91 (placeholder). Prompt 08 routes through Session/guardedWrite so emits are principal-attributed.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 16 (bootstrap)**:
  - Daemon lifecycle (start-on-boot, stop-on-shutdown) wires into bootstrap §7.
  - Observation-mode pass-through: reverse daemon does not check observation mode; forward daemon emits `xlsx.regenerated` regardless. Bootstrap wires the observation_mode_active payload field per D5.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 17 (CLI)**:
  - `adminme projection rebuild xlsx_workbooks` per BUILD.md §3.11 line 1054 (rebuild deletes both workbook files + sidecar tree, then regenerates).

- **Refactored**: by Partner in Claude Chat, 2026-04-28. Prompt file: `prompts/10b-ii-beta-thank-you-detection.md` (~330 lines, quality bar = 10b-ii-α + reuse). Secondary-split memo at `docs/02-split-memo-10b-ii.md`.

- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Evidence**:
  - `packs/skills/extract_thank_you_fields/` — full 09b-shape skill pack at v1.0.0. 4 unit tests via pack-loader canary + handler-direct (well-formed pass-through, urgency coercion, non-dict-input defensive default). Output schema's `urgency` enum matches `CommitmentProposedV1.urgency`'s Literal exactly so the pipeline round-trips without coercion drift.
  - `packs/pipelines/thank_you_detection/{pipeline.yaml,config.example.yaml,config.schema.json,handler.py,tests/test_pack_load.py}` — `ThankYouDetectionPipeline` reusing 10b-ii-α's parties-DB seam through `ctx.parties_conn_factory`; calls `classify_thank_you_candidate@^1.3.0` (already on main from 09b — NOT modified by this prompt) → `extract_thank_you_fields@^1.0.0` → emits `commitment.proposed` with `kind="other"` (above review_threshold = confident; between min and review_threshold = weak) or `commitment.suppressed` (below or on `is_candidate=False`, on skill failure, on sender resolution failure, on factory missing). Per-member overrides config-driven; tenant-agnostic placeholder member ids in `config.example.yaml`. Five inlined helpers (`_classify_identifier`, `_load_config`, `_thresholds_for_member`, `_new_commitment_id`, `_emit_suppressed`) duplicated from `commitment_extraction/handler.py` per the split memo's "no shared helper yet" guidance.

  - `tests/integration/test_pipeline_10b_ii_beta_integration.py` — 3 round-trip tests against the live runner with a seeded parties DB and monkeypatched skill runner: above-threshold emits `commitment.proposed` with `kind="other"` and causation chain intact; `is_candidate=False` with absent urgency/suggested_medium emits `commitment.suppressed` without crashing; F-5 absence-assertion confirms `messaging.sent` never derives a commitment event (its `event_id` does not appear as any `causation_id` in the log after the follow-up `messaging.received` checkpoint advances).

Suite tally on the `tests/` testpath: 464 → 480 passed, 2 skipped (the +5 pack-internal tests live under `packs/` and run via explicit path per the per-commit verification commands).
  - **F-5 carry-forward CLOSED**: `thank_you_detection` does NOT subscribe to `messaging.sent` (trigger list is `[messaging.received]` only); the handler additionally early-returns on any non-`messaging.received` event type as defense-in-depth so a future broader subscription cannot generate audit-trail noise. Different from `commitment_extraction` which subscribes to both inbound + outbound and runs outbound through the defensive-default suppression path.
  - **`kind="other"` v1 disposition CONFIRMED**: `BUILD.md §1150` depth-read concluded that "specialization of commitment extraction for gratitude" + "thank_you commitment" reads as descriptive (a commitment that is a thank-you), not as requiring a new value in `CommitmentProposedV1.kind`'s Literal. The kind enum is therefore NOT extended; thank-you commitments emit with `kind="other"` and a `classify_reasons` array conveying the thank-you signals. A future Literal-extension migration may revisit this if downstream consumers need to disambiguate.

The pipeline does not coerce/translate at the pipeline layer — the classify-side urgency is treated as a hint to the extractor only (and not even passed through, since the extractor sees the same message text). `extract_thank_you_fields` produces the canonical urgency value, which round-trips into `CommitmentProposedV1` without coercion drift.
  - `[§7.3]` (no projection direct writes): pipeline emits only via `ctx.event_log.append`; pipeline→projection canary in `verify_invariants.sh` clean.

  - `[§7.7]`: skill failure on either call does NOT raise — emits `commitment.suppressed` with `reason="skill_failure_defensive_default"`. Bus checkpoint advances normally.
  - `[D7]`: zero new event-type registrations — reuses `commitment.proposed` v1 (existing) + `commitment.suppressed` v1 (registered in 10b-ii-α).
  - `[§12.4]`: per-member-override config uses placeholder member ids (`member_id_example_*`); verify script clean.
  - `[§15]`/`[D15]`: parties-DB path resolved through `InstanceConfig.projection_db_path("parties")` in the integration test harness; no hardcoded literal in handler.
  - Causation-id wiring: every emit uses `causation_id=ctx.triggering_event_id`.
  - `verify_invariants.sh` exit 0.
  - `poetry run ruff check adminme/ packs/ tests/`: clean. (Pre-existing 2 F401 in `docs/reference/plaid/` carry forward from prior merges and are out-of-scope.)
- **Carry-forward for prompt 10c** (proactive pipelines):
  - The two-skill-chain pattern with binary-classifier-first is now established across two pipelines (`commitment_extraction`: classify-then-extract using same-prompt skill family; `thank_you_detection`: classify-then-extract with cross-prompt classifier from 09b). Future proactive pipelines that need a binary gate before extraction can clone either shape literally.

Reads `config/channels.yaml` via `load_config()` (rejects non-mapping top-level), instantiates adapters via injected `AdapterLoader` callable (PM-27: no-op default returning `[]`), spawns each as a named `asyncio.Task`, restarts crashes with exponential backoff (`INITIAL_BACKOFF_S=1.0`, `MAX_BACKOFF_S=300.0` capped, `UPTIME_RESET_S=300.0` reset threshold) per `[BUILD.md §L1]`. Emits `adapter.error` (severity `transient`) on each restart with the doubling `retry_after_s`. Optional `/status` HTTP endpoint served via stdlib `asyncio.start_server` (no aiohttp dep) on port from config (default `8081`, distinct from OpenClaw runner's `8080`; set port `0` to disable). Per `[§2.2]` NOT a projection, `[§7]` NOT a pipeline, `[§8]` invariant 3 NOT an OpenClaw plugin.
  - `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/{pack.yaml,adapter.py,config.schema.json,config.example.yaml,tests/test_gmail_adapter_pack_internal.py,tests/fixtures/{profile_response,history_response,message_alice,message_bob}.json}` — full Gmail adapter pack. Manifest: `kind: adapter`, `family: messaging`, `runtime.entrypoint: adapter.py`, `runtime.class: GmailAdapter`, `triggers.kind: external_poll` + `triggers.schedule: "*/2 * * * *"`, `depends_on.event_types: [adapter.error, adapter.authenticated, adapter.cursor_advanced, messaging.received]`. `GmailAdapter` (~640 lines): service-account OAuth2 via injected `credentials_resolver` (resolves `op://<vault>/<item>/<field>` via 1Password `op read` in production; tests inject a stub), injected `service_factory` lazily imports `google-auth`/`googleapiclient` so CI doesn't need the deps; `_resolve_sensitivity_floor` enforces `[§6]` invariant 10 (config may RAISE the floor, NEVER lower below `HARDCODED_FLOOR`); bootstraps cursor via `users.getProfile` to avoid back-flooding pre-existing mail; pages `users.history.list` until `nextPageToken` absent; dedupes `messagesAdded` across history records; fetches each message via `users.messages.get(format=full)`; BFS the MIME tree to extract `text/plain` + `text/html` bodies (tolerates missing base64url padding); emits `messaging.received` v1 per added message; `_maybe_emit_cursor_advance` gates emission on the 100-event-OR-5-minute milestone per anti-vision §"Quiet by default"; `_classify_gmail_error` duck-types `googleapiclient.errors.HttpError` (reads `.resp.status` + `Retry-After` header) without importing the SDK at load time. Defensive 7-tuple-equivalent catch in `_poll_loop` (`BaseException`-classified) emits `adapter.error` and routes transient → continue, fatal → halt for supervisor restart.
  - `config/channels.yaml` (NEW) — minimal example with the Gmail adapter as the single entry; `adapter_supervisor_port: 8081`, `adapter_supervisor_host: 127.0.0.1`. Wizard (prompt 16) owns programmatic writes; until then operators edit by hand. Secret references are 1Password (`op://AdministrateMe/gmail-sandbox/service-account-json`).
  - Pre-scaffold cleanup (c1): deleted the nine `adminme/adapters/{calendaring,contacts,documents,financial,iot,messaging,reminders,telephony,webhook}/` placeholder directories from prompt 02 per `docs/06-split-memo-11.md` §4 (operator decision: adapter implementations live in `packs/adapters/` not `adminme/adapters/`). Each directory contained only an empty `__init__.py`; verified before deletion. `ls adminme/adapters/` now shows only `__init__.py` + `base.py`.
  - **Tests: 27 new across the four commits** (target floor was 18; overshoot welcome): c1 ships 16 (3 schema canaries + 5 base.py Protocol + AdapterContext + AdapterStatus + sensitivity-floor literal + adapter-state literal + 8 supervisor — config parsing + lifecycle clean shutdown + restart-on-crash with backoff + constants); c2 ships 6 (1 pack-load canary + 1 Protocol satisfaction + 2 service-account auth + 3 sensitivity-floor invariant including raise-from-normal-to-privileged + downgrade-refusal + unknown-value-refusal); c3 ships 6 (1 bootstrap-via-getProfile + 1 history-sync-emits-messaging-received-per-added-message + 3 cursor-milestone — event-threshold + skipped-below-milestone + time-threshold + 1 rate-limit-transient + 1 auth-failure-fatal); c4 ships 5 integration (full round-trip lifecycle + restart-on-crash + real `config/channels.yaml` parse + `/status` HTTP endpoint snapshot + correlation_id propagation through emitted events). Total suite expansion: pack-internal 8 (under `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/tests/`) + unit 16 (under `tests/unit/`) + integration 5 (under `tests/integration/`).
  - `[§7.3]` / `[D17]` (no projection writes): the supervisor and the Gmail adapter both write only via `ctx.event_log.append_sync` (or async `append` when present); `verify_invariants.sh` pipeline→projection canary clean.
  - `[§8.2]` / `[D6]` (no LLM SDK in adapters): zero `from anthropic` / `from openai` imports anywhere under `packs/adapters/` or `adminme/adapters/` or `adminme/daemons/`. `googleapiclient` + `google.oauth2.service_account` are imported lazily inside `_default_service_factory` (not at module load) so CI passes without them.
  - `[§8]` invariant 3 (channel transport is OpenClaw's): the Gmail adapter only emits events; no outbound calls (Telegram client, BlueBubbles client, etc.) anywhere in the pack.
  - `[§6]` invariant 10 (sensitivity-floor downgrade refused): `_resolve_sensitivity_floor` raises on any config that would lower the class-level `HARDCODED_FLOOR`. Three tests assert the raise/downgrade/unknown-value paths.
  - `[D7]`: three new event types register at v1; `model_config = {"extra": "forbid"}` on all three.
  - `[D8]`: every emitted event carries `correlation_id=ctx.correlation_id_factory()` — the supervisor mints a fresh id per managed-adapter start cycle; integration test asserts every emission's correlation_id matches the supervisor's minted sequence.
  - `[§12.4]` (tenant-identity firewall): all test fixtures use `sandbox@example.test`/`alice@example.test`/`bob@example.test` placeholders; no real tenant identifiers in `config/channels.yaml` or any pack file (the placeholder `service_account_key_ref` references the `AdministrateMe` vault namespace which is a project-not-person identifier).
  - F-3 (pack-internal pack-load test rename) applied: `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/tests/test_gmail_adapter_pack_internal.py` uses a unique basename so collection from the repo root does not collide with sibling adapter packs landing in 11b/11c/11d.
  - `verify_invariants.sh` exit 0; `poetry run ruff check adminme/ packs/ tests/` clean; `poetry run mypy adminme/` clean.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11b** (Plaid financial adapter):
  - Clone the pack shape from `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/` literally → `packs/adapters/financial/plaid/`. The `Adapter` Protocol from `adminme.adapters.base`, the three cross-cutting schemas in `adminme/events/schemas/adapters.py`, and the supervisor's lifecycle wiring are all reusable as-is. `cursor_kind` for Plaid is `transactions_cursor` (vs Gmail's `history_id`). Plaid emits an ingest-domain event (likely `financial.transaction_observed` or similar) instead of `messaging.received` — that schema lands in 11b's c1, not here.

The bidirectional surface (Reminders adapter also WRITES to Apple Reminders) is new — neither Gmail nor Plaid does outbound. Decision deferred to 11c: either add a `commit()` method to the Protocol (most adapters no-op) or route writes through a separate skill pack that the adapter consumes. Lean toward the latter to preserve `[§8]` invariant 3 (channel transport is OpenClaw's).

Google Calendar reuses the Gmail service-account auth pattern (same `_default_credentials_resolver` + `_default_service_factory` shape — likely worth extracting to `adminme/adapters/_google_auth.py` once both adapters land). CalDAV is HTTP basic + a CalDAV client library (deferred dep choice).

The conceptual shape (cursor persistence, error emission, auth-on-startup) is similar but the runtime is different per `[BUILD.md §L1]`'s two-runtime distinction. Document the boundary in 12's prompt; do not try to unify the two runtimes.

The wizard also needs an `adapter_install_order.yaml` (analogous to `bootstrap/pack_install_order.yaml` for skill packs); adapter packs are NOT added to the existing skill-pack file per its docstring.
- **Carry-forward — soft watch for Pub/Sub push notifications**:
  - The Gmail adapter implements polling (history API) only in 11a. The Gmail Pub/Sub push-notification receiver path (`users.watch` + a webhook) is a deferred optimization. Future sidecar after the polling baseline is proven; no specific prompt assigned. The supervisor's existing lifecycle already handles webhook receivers in principle (a webhook adapter is just an adapter whose `start()` registers an HTTP listener and yields events back to the log).
- **Carry-forward — soft watch F-PB-1 (10d audit)**:
  - `bootstrap/pack_install_order.yaml` currently lists 1 of 10 skill packs. Prompt 11a does NOT extend that file — adapter packs need their own `bootstrap/adapter_install_order.yaml` per prompt 16. The F-PB-1 gap remains; carry forward to whichever bootstrap-wiring prompt resolves it (currently scoped to 16).

One SUMMARY event per `/transactions/sync` cycle (NEVER per transaction) per VISION §"Quiet by default".

Class attrs `name="financial-plaid"`, `family="financial"`, `version="1.0.0"`, `sensitivity_floor="privileged"`. `start()` resolves credentials via `op://` references (rejects non-op-shape per `[§L1 token-storage discipline]`), instantiates Plaid client via `plaid_client_factory` (PM-27 default no-op), emits `adapter.authenticated` v1, runs the startup institution-health probe, then enters the daily-backstop poll loop that wraps `await self._stop_event.wait()` in `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=interval_s)`. `webhook_handler()` verifies the JWT via `webhook_verifier_factory` (PM-27 default permissive for tests), dispatches `SYNC_UPDATES_AVAILABLE` → `_sync_transactions(..., webhook_triggered=True)` and `ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED` → `adapter.error` `severity=fatal` `error_kind=auth_failed`. `_sync_transactions()` paginates while `has_more`, persists the post-pagination cursor at the `(member_id, plaid_item_id)` granularity per `BUILD.md PLAID DETAILED SPEC §"Sync cursor"`, emits one `money_flow.plaid_synced` per cycle, and emits `adapter.cursor_advanced` only at milestones (every 100 events OR every 5 minutes per the cross-cutting discipline). `_fetch_institution_health()` calls `/institutions/get_by_id` with `options.include_status=True` per `docs/reference/plaid/institutions-api.md`; any non-`HEALTHY` per-product status emits `adapter.error` `severity=transient` `error_kind=api_changed`; SDK-level failures classify through the same 7-tuple as `/transactions/sync`. Defensive 7-tuple catch (`TransientError`, `RateLimitError`, `NetworkError`, `APIChangedError`, `QuotaExceededError`, `AuthError`, `UnknownError`) mirrors the `compose_morning_digest` pattern from 10c-ii. `AuthError` is the only `fatal` entry — re-auth requires the prompt-16 wizard.

### Prompt 11c — Apple Reminders bidirectional adapter
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-16. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11c-adapter-reminders-bidirectional.md` (~297 lines, quality bar = 11b). Third sub-prompt of the prompt-11 Tier C split per `docs/06-split-memo-11.md`; ships the FIRST bidirectional adapter (inbound iCloud→event-log + outbound event-log→iCloud) cloning the 11a Gmail-adapter-pack shape with Reminders-specific substitutions.
- **Session merged**: PRs #108 / #109 / #110 / #111 (squash-merged sequentially), c1-c4 main shas `b295760` / `7c66496` / `e0b6ee4` / `9117ed3`, merged 2026-05-16. Wrong-canon per memo 08; Cohort C step 3 (11r-c) will retrofit Reminders onto ExternalStateMirrorAdapter with `list.*` / `list_item.*` schema migration per [D18].
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Evidence**:
  - `adminme/events/schemas/reminders.py` (NEW) — `ReminderListItemSyncedV1` registered at v1 per `[D7]`. Fields: `member_id`, `remind_id` (Apple's `calendarItemIdentifier`), `list_identifier`, `list_name` (all `min_length=1`); `title` (`max_length=500`), `notes` (`max_length=2000` or `None`), `due_at` (`datetime` or `None`), `priority` (`Literal[0, 1, 5, 9]` matching EventKit's none/high/medium/low ordinals), `completed` (`bool`), `last_modified_at` (`datetime`), `direction` (`Literal["inbound", "outbound"]`), `sync_cycle_id` (`min_length=1`). `model_config = {"extra": "forbid"}`. One event per genuinely-new or modified reminder; NEVER per unchanged poll-cycle entry per anti-vision §"Quiet by default".
  - `packs/adapters/reminders/apple-eventkit/` (NEW pack, third L1 adapter, FIRST bidirectional worked example):
    - `pack.yaml` — `kind: adapter`, `family: reminders`, `version: 1.0.0`, `sensitivity_floor: normal` (constant). `triggers.kind: external_poll`, `triggers.schedule: "*/1 * * * *"` (1-minute supervisor heartbeat; the in-process active cadence is 30s in BOTH directions per BUILD.md APPLE REMINDERS BIDIRECTIONAL §"Polling cadence" + §"Outbound write cadence"). `depends_on.event_types` declares 7 types — 3 cross-cutting `adapter.*` + `reminder.list_item.synced` + 3 outbound triggers (`reminder.dispatched`, `task.created`, `commitment.proposed`). `events_emitted` documents the 4 types this adapter actually emits.
    - `config.schema.json` — JSON schema with `"sensitivity_floor": {"const": "normal"}` per `[§6 invariant 10]` so JSON-schema rejects any attempt to raise the floor to `"privileged"`. `additionalProperties: false`. Required: `target_list_identifier`, `member_id`. Optional with defaults: `never_sync_list_substrings: ["Work", "Case", "Client"]`, `never_sync_tag_substrings: ["#private"]`, `inbound_poll_interval_s: 30`, `outbound_poll_interval_s: 30`.
    - `config.example.yaml` — placeholder list identifiers + the canonical never-sync defaults. NO `op://` credential refs — EventKit access is granted at the OS level via `EKEventStore.requestFullAccessToReminders` per `docs/reference/apple-eventkit/access.md`; the prompt-16 bootstrap-wizard owns the grant flow.
    - `never_sync.py` (NEW; the predicate as a standalone module so c2 + c3 + integration tests import + verify it in isolation). `passes_never_sync(list_name, title, notes, member_role, never_sync_list_substrings, never_sync_tag_substrings)` returns `True` iff ALL three canonical rules pass: list-name substring (case-insensitive, default `["Work", "Case", "Client"]`), item-tag substring (case-insensitive, default `["#private"]`, matched on title+notes), owning member role NOT in `{"child", "ambient"}`. The predicate is pure — no projection reads, no skill calls, no LLM per `[§8.2, D6]`.
    - `_eventkit_shim.py` (NEW) — Protocol-shaped `EKEventStoreLike` + `EKReminderLike` covering the four-operation surface the adapter relies on (`build_reminder`, `save`, `fetch_reminders`, `list_name_for_identifier`). `NoopEKEventStore` default raises on every call so any production path without a real factory wired fails loudly. Per Assumption A8: production wires `pyobjc-framework-EventKit` (or equivalent) at the prompt-16 bootstrap-wizard; tests substitute strict fakes via constructor injection per PM-27.
    - `adapter.py` — implements the `Adapter` Protocol from `adminme/adapters/base.py`. Class attrs `name="reminders-apple-eventkit"`, `family="reminders"`, `version="1.0.0"`, `sensitivity_floor="normal"`. `__init__` re-asserts the class-level floor against `config["sensitivity_floor"]` (rejects anything other than `"normal"`). `start()` validates `target_list_identifier` + `member_id`, instantiates the EKEventStore via the injected factory, emits `adapter.authenticated` v1 with `token_storage_ref="os://reminders-permission"` (NOT an `op://` ref — EventKit grant lives at the OS layer), spawns BOTH the outbound poll task AND the inbound poll task via `asyncio.create_task`, and `asyncio.gather`s them so a single `stop()` cancels both cleanly. `stop()` is idempotent. `_outbound_poll` drains the queued upstream events (`reminder.dispatched` / `task.created` / `commitment.proposed`) on each 30s tick, translates each into an `EKReminder` field bundle via `_translate_upstream`, applies `passes_never_sync` at the OUTBOUND boundary, calls `EKEventStore.save(_:commit:)`, and emits `reminder.list_item.synced` v1 with `direction="outbound"` + `causation_id` set to the upstream event id (round-trip traceability). `_inbound_poll` calls `_fetch_reminders_filtered` (which applies `passes_never_sync` at the INBOUND boundary symmetric with outbound), `_is_new_or_modified` (dedupe: session cache > projection resolver > new), and emits `reminder.list_item.synced` v1 with `direction="inbound"` + `causation_id=None` (iCloud is the source — no upstream AdministrateMe event). `_maybe_emit_inbound_cursor_advance` emits `adapter.cursor_advanced` v1 at the 100-event-OR-5-minute milestone shared with Gmail + Plaid; the `cursor_value` packs the entire per-list `(list_identifier → last_modified_at)` watermark dict as a JSON-encoded string so a single audit event captures the whole reminder-family cursor map. Defensive `BaseException`-classifying catch in BOTH poll loops mirrors the Gmail + Plaid pattern: `_classify_eventkit_error` maps `permission`/`auth`-class exception names → `("fatal", "auth_failed")`, network/timeout → `("transient", "network")`, everything else → `("transient", "unknown")`. `transient` keeps the loop going (state → `"backoff"`); `fatal` halts (state → `"crashed"`) for the supervisor's restart-with-backoff window.
    - `tests/test_apple_eventkit_adapter_pack_internal.py` (F-3 unique basename) — 24 pack-internal tests: 1 manifest-load canary, 1 Protocol-satisfaction canary, 1 sensitivity-floor-cannot-be-raised, 3 outbound (drain calls save, non-trigger types ignored, sync emits `direction="outbound"` + causation), 2 outbound never-sync (Work-list blocked + child-member-role blocked), 6 inbound (fixture-driven emit-3-of-4, 200-Work-reminders → 0 events, item-tag blocks `#private`, dedupe skips unchanged second poll, dedupe re-emits on `last_modified_at` advance, permissive-resolver-fallback), 1 inbound dedupe with projection resolver (emit vs. skip arms), 2 inbound cursor (100-event milestone fires + sub-100 silence), 1 inbound poll-loop bounded via `max_inbound_cycles`, AND the 2 new c4 supervisor-lifecycle tests (both-poll-tasks spawned + cancelled cleanly; `NoopEKEventStore` raises → `adapter.error` v1 in the supervisor-expected shape).
    - `tests/fixtures/inbound_basic.json` — recorded EventKit shape with 4 reminders across 4 lists (Household, Family, Work case files, Personal); the Personal-list `"#private — therapy appt"` reminder exercises the tag-substring rule; the Work case files list exercises the list-name substring rule when expanded to 200 reminders.

  - `tests/unit/adapters/test_never_sync_predicate.py` (NEW) — 6 never-sync predicate tests: list-name substring case-insensitive match, item-tag in title, item-tag in notes, child-role excluded, ambient-role excluded, adult role + clean list + clean text → True. Predicate purity asserted by direct call (no event log, no projection).
  - `tests/integration/test_adapter_reminders_supervisor.py` (NEW, `pytestmark = pytest.mark.requires_live_services`) — 3 supervisor-lifecycle round-trips: (a) inbound — supervisor spawns adapter, bounded inbound cycle emits 3 `reminder.list_item.synced` with `direction="inbound"` + no `causation_id` against the fixture (1 of 4 fixture reminders blocked by `#private` tag); (b) outbound — pre-queued `reminder.dispatched` drains through the bounded outbound cycle, `EKEventStore.save(_:commit:)` called once with `commit=True`, one synced event emitted with `direction="outbound"` and `causation_id` set to the upstream event id; (c) on-disk `config/channels.yaml` parses and exposes the new `reminders-apple-eventkit` entry alongside the existing Gmail + Plaid entries.

The new entry carries `target_list_identifier: "AdministrateMe Household"`, the canonical never-sync defaults, and the 30s in-process polling cadences for both directions.

The 5 ABCs extend the simple `Adapter` Protocol from `adminme/adapters/base.py` (PR #91 series) with category-specific shape; the Protocol stays unchanged per §6 out-of-scope discipline.
    - `communication_base.py` — `CommunicationAdapter` (Cat-A). `family: Literal["messaging"]` pinned at class level; `capabilities` ClassVar with ≥1 Cat-A entry required by subclasses; abstract `send(message: OutboundMessage) -> SendResult` and `_on_inbound(envelope: InboundEnvelope) -> None`. The base class **declares** the §6.20 observation-mode-wrapping contract; the 11-β validator verifies subclass adherence (`send` MUST route through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound`). Frozen dataclasses for the three envelope types.
    - `external_state_mirror_base.py` — `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter` (Cat-B). The most complex of the 5 ABCs because it carries the write surface. `family: Literal["reminders", "calendaring", "contacts"]`; `write_capabilities: ClassVar[list[str]]` per-operation list per `[UT-27]`; `sharing_model_discriminator: ClassVar[str]` naming the per-member-vs-shared discriminator column on the Cat-B projection per memo 05 §2.5. `apply_write` is CONCRETE on the base class — it checks operation membership against `write_capabilities` and raises `WriteCapabilityError` BEFORE invoking the subclass-provided `_apply_write_impl`. The asymmetric per-operation gating per `[UT-27]` is centralized in the ABC so every Cat-B adapter inherits the check uniformly.
    - `inbound_data_base.py` — `InboundDataAdapter` (Cat-C). The simplest of the 5 ABCs by design — Cat-C is the discriminator from Cat-B (no write surface). `family: Literal["financial", "iot", "telemetry"]`; abstract `start` / `stop` / `status` from the simple Protocol; **no** `apply_write` / `send` / `execute_action`. The absence of `write_capabilities` ClassVar surfaces the read-only-by-shape contract; the 11-β validator rejects any Cat-C pack manifest declaring `write_capabilities` per `[UT-27]`.
    - `personal_knowledge_base.py` — `PersonalKnowledgeAdapter` (Cat-D). `family: Literal["knowledge"]` pinned; `runtime: Literal["bridge"] = "bridge"` pinned per memo 04 §2.1 (Cat-D is bridge-only); `_owner_scope_immutable: Final[bool] = True` surfaces the Tailscale-identity-binds-owner-scope invariant from memo 04 §2.2 (the `:3337 bridge` ingest endpoint stamps `owner_scope = private:<member_id>` from the resolved Tailscale identity — the adapter MUST NOT participate in setting it). The `Final` flag is the authorship-time enforcement point; the ingest endpoint is the runtime enforcement point; the 11-β validator additionally pairs this with `owner_scope_overridability_at_install=False` on the manifest.
    - `outbound_action_base.py` — `OutboundActionAdapter` (Cat-E). `family: Literal["home_automation", "telephony", "external_action"]`; `observation_mode_required: ClassVar[bool] = True` (Cat-E adapters MUST route `execute_action` through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` per `[§6.20]`; the validator rejects any Cat-E pack manifest setting `observation_mode_required=False`). Abstract `execute_action(request: ActionRequest) -> ActionResult` returns an `ActionResult` carrying `suppressed=True` when observation mode short-circuits the external dispatch.
    - All 5 ABCs use `abc.ABC` + `abstractmethod` so missing implementations fail at instantiation, not silently at call time. Each file lands well under the §1 anti-vision halt's 200-line ceiling (Cat-A ~155 lines, Cat-B ~176 lines, Cat-C ~81 lines, Cat-D ~107 lines, Cat-E ~131 lines).
  - `adminme/lib/pack_registry/` (NEW package — sibling to `adminme/lib/skill_runner/`, not a fork; adapter packs get parallel infrastructure per the Gate 2 path correction in prompt §1):

    - `models.py` — Pydantic models per memo 05 §3.1 / `[PM-33]` / `[PM-35]`. `Capability` (frozen, `extra="forbid"`) carries `kind: Literal["Cat-A" .. "Cat-E"]`, `runtime: Literal["central" | "bridge" | "dual" | "plugin"]`, optional `event_families` / `sensitivity_default` / `observation_mode_required` per-capability overrides. `AdapterManifest` (`extra="forbid"`) extends the existing flat pack.yaml model with `capabilities: list[Capability]` (multi-capability shape per `[PM-35]`), `write_capabilities: list[str]` (per-operation strings per `[UT-27]`), `sensitivity_default` (top-level; richer 3-level ladder `normal | sensitive | privileged` per memo 05 §2.4 — strictly richer than the simple Protocol's 2-level `normal | privileged` floor), `observation_mode_required` boolean, `owner_scope_overridability_at_install` boolean (default True; Cat-D packs set False), `sharing_model_discriminator: str | None`, `dedup_strategy: dict | None` per `[PM-31]`. The `resolve_effective_sensitivity(capability_index)` helper clamps per-capability overrides at the pack-level floor per `[§6 invariant 10]` — a per-capability override BELOW the pack default is ignored, never honoured, because the pack-level floor is the strictest writeable bound across the pack's capabilities.
    - `parser.py` — `parse_adapter_manifest(pack_dir: Path) -> AdapterManifest`. Mirrors the `skill_runner/pack_loader.py` pattern (yaml.safe_load + Pydantic validation) but is structurally separate (adapter packs are not skill packs; the loader-level fields are different). The parser extracts only the recognized **extension** fields (`_EXTENSION_FIELDS`) from pack.yaml and forwards them to `AdapterManifest`; existing top-level fields (`pack:` / `runtime:` / `family:` / `triggers:` / etc.) are left to the existing loader / supervisor. Backward-compat: legacy v1 manifests (without `capabilities:`) parse cleanly with `capabilities=[]` default. `ManifestParseError` distinct from `PackLoadError` so adapter-pack and skill-pack failure modes don't conflate.
