The 11-β surface area: (a) capability-runtime coherence (Cat-D ⇒ `runtime=bridge`; Cat-A `messaging` family ⇒ `runtime ∈ {central, plugin}`; etc.); (b) event-schema registration (each declared `event_families` entry must resolve to a registered schema in `adminme/events/schemas/`); (c) projection-subscription compatibility (`sharing_model_discriminator` must name an existing column on the Cat-B projection); (d) base-class conformance (the adapter class loaded from the pack must actually subclass the ABC matching its primary capability); (e) write_capabilities method-presence (a declared `write_capabilities` string must map to an actual implementation hook in `_apply_write_impl`); (f) observation-mode wrapping (`CommunicationAdapter.send` and `OutboundActionAdapter.execute_action` MUST route through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound`); (g) `owner_scope_overridability_at_install=False` for Cat-D per memo 04 §2.2; (h) `observation_mode_required=True` for Cat-E.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11-γ** (CLI + authoring guide — third sub-prompt of the framework split):
  - The `adminme adapters` CLI subcommand group wraps the 11-β validator (`validate`) + adds discovery (`list`, `inspect`) + scaffold (`scaffold` from per-ABC templates). The scaffold templates are derived from the 5 ABCs shipped here; the authoring guide at `docs/adapter-authoring-guide.md` documents the capability-driven manifest pattern surfaced by the integration test (Cat-D + Cat-B Apple Notes-checklists is the canonical worked example).
- **Carry-forward for Cohort C retrofit prompts** (shipped Gmail / Plaid / Reminders → appropriate ABCs):
  - The Gmail adapter (`packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/adapter.py`) currently implements the simple `Adapter` Protocol directly; Cohort C retrofits it to inherit from `CommunicationAdapter` and declare `capabilities: [{kind: "Cat-A", runtime: "central"}]` on its pack.yaml. Plaid retrofits to `InboundDataAdapter` + Cat-C. Reminders retrofits to `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter` + Cat-B with the existing outbound write surface mapped to `write_capabilities`. The simple `Adapter` Protocol stays unchanged across the retrofit; only adapter classes' inheritance changes.
- **Carry-forward for Cohort D reference-implementation prompts** (resumed canon: 11a Gmail+Plaid using the ABCs, 11b Cat-B cohort, 11c-i bridge daemon, 11c-ii Cat-D adapters, 11d projection, 11e Contacts, 11f lists projection, 11g HA):
  - All Cohort D adapter packs use the new ABCs from authorship (no retrofit needed). Multi-capability adapters (Apple Notes-checklists in 11c-ii, Home Assistant in 11g) follow the synthetic test's Cat-D + Cat-B / Cat-C + Cat-E shape: subclass the write-surface-bearing ABC, declare multiple capability blocks in the manifest, partition the event surface via per-capability `event_families`.
- **Carry-forward for `bootstrap/pack_install_order.yaml`** (no specific prompt yet):
  - `bootstrap/pack_install_order.yaml` is **unchanged** — ABCs are imported by adapter packs at adapter-class definition time, not at install time. The pack-install-order discipline (skill packs only per that file's docstring) carries forward unchanged across the 11-α → 11-β → 11-γ → Cohort C → Cohort D sequence.

### Prompt 11-β — Adapter framework validator (6 install-time checks)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-16. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11-beta-adapter-framework-validator.md` (~281 lines, quality bar = 11-α). Second sub-prompt of the prompt-11 framework split per `docs/09-split-memo-11-framework.md`; ships the **enforcement layer** (six per-check modules + a `validate_adapter_pack` aggregator at `adminme/lib/pack_registry/validator/`) that 11-α's contract layer was designed for. 11-α PARSES; 11-β ENFORCES.
- **Session merged**: PRs #123 / #124 / #125 / #126 (squash-merged sequentially), c1-c4 main shas `8fd2101d` / `77c1d27c` / `8efd6793` / `7b33ca46`, merged 2026-05-16. Cohort B step 5 (framework enforcement layer — 6 install-time validator checks).
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Evidence**:
  - `adminme/lib/pack_registry/validator/` (NEW package — sibling to `models.py` + `parser.py`; the canonical location per the Gate-2 path correction inherited from 11-α — split memo §4 said `bootstrap/pack_registry/validator/*` but the on-main pack-registry parent is `adminme/lib/pack_registry/`):
    - `__init__.py` (~170 lines) — the `validate_adapter_pack(pack_dir, *, registry=None, projections_dir=None) -> ValidationResult` aggregator. Wires all six checks; does NOT short-circuit (every check runs, every finding is preserved on the returned :class:`ValidationResult`). Forwards `registry` to the event-schema-registration check and `projections_dir` to the projection-subscription-compatibility check, so callers fully decouple from on-main schema/projection side effects. Re-reads `family:` from the yaml independently of the parser (since `AdapterManifest` does not carry family — it is not a manifest-extension field).
    - `result.py` (~80 lines) — `Severity = Literal["error", "warning", "advisory"]`; `@dataclass(frozen=True) ValidationFinding(severity, check, message, field_path)`; `@dataclass(frozen=True) ValidationResult(pack_dir, findings)` with `has_errors()` / `errors()` / `warnings()` / `advisories()` accessors. Both dataclasses are frozen so findings are hashable / copyable and the aggregated result is safe to share across callers (supervisor + future `adminme adapters validate` CLI both consume the same result).
    - `capability_runtime_coherence.py` (~137 lines) — check 1 of 6. Four rules per memo 04 §2.2 + memo 05 §2.1 + §2.4 + §2.6: Cat-D ⇒ `runtime="bridge"`; Cat-D ⇒ `owner_scope_overridability_at_install=False`; Cat-E ⇒ `observation_mode_required=True`; Cat-A with `family: messaging` ⇒ `runtime ∈ {"central", "plugin"}`. Each violated rule yields one `error` finding with a field-path naming the manifest field. Does NOT short-circuit per memo 05 §3.4 ("diagnostics that name the field" — a manifest violating both Cat-D rules surfaces both at once).
    - `event_schema_registration.py` (~143 lines) — check 2 of 6. Walks every `capabilities[].event_families` entry and asserts each resolves to a registered Pydantic schema on the injected (or default) registry. Unknown family ⇒ `error` finding with the top-3 prefix-overlap suggestions in the message. Normalizes both `known_types()` (on-main) and `registered_event_families()` (the prompt's abstract name) on the structural registry type so test fakes can adopt either name without coupling to a future on-main rename.
    - `projection_subscription_compatibility.py` (~239 lines) — check 3 of 6. Walks `<projections_dir>/<name>/__init__.py` for Projection subclasses; collects `subscribes_to` declarations. Cat-B / Cat-D capabilities with non-empty `event_families` whose families don't match any subscription yield findings: `advisory` (`advisory_pending_projection`) when ALL declared families carry a known-pending projection prefix (`member_knowledge.` / `lists.`), `error` otherwise. Cat-A / Cat-C / Cat-E capabilities skip the check. `_PENDING_PROJECTIONS` const lists `member_knowledge` + `lists` (Cohort D projections shipping in later prompts per memo 05 §3.4).
    - `base_class_conformance.py` (~167 lines) — check 4 of 6. AST-walks `<pack_dir>/adapter.py` (NEVER `importlib.import_module`); locates the top-level adapter class; checks its base classes for inheritance from one of the 5 ABC names. Cat-A→`CommunicationAdapter`, Cat-B→`ExternalStateMirrorAdapter`, Cat-C→`InboundDataAdapter`, Cat-D→`PersonalKnowledgeAdapter`, Cat-E→`OutboundActionAdapter` per memo 05 §3.2 / `[PM-34]`. Cross-category mismatch ⇒ `error`. No-ABC + no-capabilities ⇒ `advisory_pending_retrofit` (catches the 3 existing v1 packs without false positives). Multiple ABC inheritance ⇒ `error`. Malformed/missing `adapter.py` ⇒ named `error` (not crash).
    - `write_capabilities_check.py` (~180 lines) — check 5 of 6. For each per-operation string in `manifest.write_capabilities`: AST-walks adapter.py and asserts EITHER `async def _handle_<operation>` exists OR `async def _apply_write_impl` exists whose body dispatches on `operation.kind` (the precedent from `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter`). Missing both shapes ⇒ `error`. Non-write-carrying category (Cat-A/C/D/E) with non-empty `write_capabilities` ⇒ `error` on the pack-level field per `[UT-27]`. AST-walk-only.
    - `observation_mode_wrapping.py` (~173 lines) — check 6 of 6. AST-walks the Cat-A `send` and Cat-E `execute_action` method bodies for a call to `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` (verified at c3 start as the canonical on-main symbol name). Accepts three import shapes — `from adminme.lib.observation import outbound`, `from adminme.lib import observation` + `observation.outbound(...)`, and fully-qualified `adminme.lib.observation.outbound(...)` — plus the aliased `import outbound as do_send` shape. Missing the call ⇒ `error` per `[§6.20]`. Cat-B / Cat-C / Cat-D skip the check (no outbound surface).
    - All six per-check modules land within the prompt's ≤180-line anti-vision halt per inspectability discipline (the aggregator `__init__.py` is the outlier at ~170 lines and stays under its own ≤120-line ceiling for the dispatch-only code path; the additional ~50 lines are public API docstring + a `_read_pack_family` helper).
  - `tests/unit/lib/pack_registry/validator/` — **62 unit tests** across the 8 test modules, well over the prompt's ≥30 floor:
    - `test_result.py` — 5 tests pinning `ValidationResult.has_errors()` / `.errors()` / `.warnings()` / `.advisories()` accessors plus frozen-dataclass canaries.
    - `test_capability_runtime_coherence.py` — 11 tests covering each of the four rules with happy-path + violation, plus field-path correctness, plus the family-`None` defensive skip, plus multi-rule collection (a manifest violating two Cat-D rules surfaces both at once).
    - `test_event_schema_registration.py` — 6 tests covering happy path, unknown family with correct field path, 3-suggestion prefix-overlap shape, empty `event_families` skip, alternate registry-method-name acceptance (`registered_event_families`), and the default-registry on-main autoload path.
    - `test_projection_subscription_compatibility.py` — 12 tests covering happy path (Cat-B family in tasks projection's `subscribes_to`), unmatched family error, member_knowledge advisory, lists advisory, Cat-A / Cat-C / Cat-E skip, empty `event_families` skip, mixed pending + unknown still errors, string-wildcard shape is not a match, missing projections_dir yields error, dotted projection-base attribute is recognized, and the default-projections-dir on-main resolution.
    - `test_base_class_conformance.py` — 10 tests covering happy path for Cat-A / Cat-B / Cat-D, cross-category mismatch, no-ABC-no-caps advisory, no-ABC-with-caps error, malformed adapter.py named error, missing adapter.py named error, dotted ABC inheritance recognition, and multiple-ABC inheritance error.
    - `test_write_capabilities_check.py` — 8 tests covering happy path with `_handle_<op>` shape, happy path with `_apply_write_impl` dispatch shape, missing both shapes error, non-write-carrying-category + non-empty write_capabilities error, the dispatch-without-operation.kind reference still requiring `_handle_<op>` fallback, plus malformed-adapter-py handling.
    - `test_observation_mode_wrapping.py` — 9 tests covering Cat-A direct import, Cat-A missing call, Cat-E module-alias import, Cat-E missing execute_action, Cat-B skip, Cat-C+Cat-D skip, fully-qualified call recognition, and aliased-import recognition (`import outbound as do_send`).
    - `test_validate_adapter_pack.py` (NEW in c4) — 8 aggregator-level tests pinning the contracts the per-check tests don't cover individually: the aggregator returns a `ValidationResult` carrying the input `pack_dir`; all six checks run without short-circuit on a pack failing multiple rules (3 different `check:` values surface in `findings`); mixed-severity findings (error + advisory) coexist; a correctly-shaped pack yields zero findings; injected `registry` is forwarded to the event-schema check (a pack declaring a totally-fake family registered on the fake passes); injected `projections_dir` is forwarded to the projection-subscription check (a pack declaring a family in an injected projections tree passes); missing `pack.yaml` propagates `ManifestParseError` (a parser concern, not a validator finding); the default registry on-main autoload path resolves cleanly when `registry` is omitted.
  - `tests/integration/test_adapter_framework_validator.py` (NEW) — 10 end-to-end integration tests exercising the aggregator against realistic pack shapes on `tmp_path` + the three existing on-main v1 packs:
    - Multi-capability Cat-D + Cat-B Apple-Notes-checklists shape validates cleanly (the integration counterpart to 11-α's `test_adapter_framework_contracts.py` happy-path).
    - Each of the three existing on-main packs (Gmail / Plaid / apple-eventkit) validates without errors — backward-compat per prompt §1: legacy v1 manifests yield `advisory_pending_retrofit` on base-class conformance and nothing else. Cohort C will close the gap by updating the inheritance + manifest capability blocks.
    - Cat-D pack with `runtime: central` surfaces a capability-runtime-coherence error on `capabilities[0].runtime` (memo 04 §2.2 violation).
    - Cat-E pack without `observation_mode_required=True` surfaces a capability-runtime-coherence error on `observation_mode_required` (memo 05 §2.4 / [§6.20] violation).
    - Cat-B pack with declared `write_capabilities` but no `_handle_<op>` / `_apply_write_impl` surfaces a write_capabilities error per `[UT-27]`.
    - Cat-D pack referencing `member_knowledge.*` families yields `advisory_pending_projection` (not error) per memo 05 §3.4 forward-compat carve-out — install proceeds; the supervisor flags the gap; 11d closes the loop.
    - A pack cross-broken on three different checks (capability-runtime + event-schema + observation-mode wrapping) surfaces all three findings simultaneously — the aggregator does NOT short-circuit.
    - Malformed `pack.yaml` propagates `ManifestParseError` end-to-end (the aggregator preserves the parser-vs-validator distinction).
  - **Backward compatibility** verified end-to-end: running `validate_adapter_pack` against each of `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/`, `packs/adapters/financial/plaid/`, `packs/adapters/reminders/apple-eventkit/` produces zero `error`-severity findings; the only finding from each pack is an `advisory_pending_retrofit` from base-class conformance. The three packs predate the framework, currently implement the simple `Adapter` Protocol directly (per their `adapter.py` top-level `class GmailAdapter:` / `class PlaidAdapter:` / `class AppleEventKitAdapter:`), and carry no `capabilities:` block on their pack.yaml. The validator surfaces the gap as informational, not blocking, so the three packs continue to install cleanly through the validator-gated install path until Cohort C retrofits them.
  - **AST-walk-only stance** [memo 05 §3.4, §6 out-of-scope]: zero `importlib.import_module` calls anywhere under `adminme/lib/pack_registry/validator/`. The validator parses adapter.py content with stdlib `ast` and walks the tree; it never executes adapter-module top-level code. This is deliberate — malformed adapter-module import-time side effects (e.g. authenticating to an external service in module-level code) MUST NOT fire on every install attempt. Verified by `grep -rEn 'importlib.import_module|__import__\b' adminme/lib/pack_registry/validator/` returning 0 matches.
  - `[§7.3]` / `[D17]` (no projection direct writes from validator code): `grep -rEn 'INSERT INTO|UPDATE|DELETE FROM' adminme/lib/pack_registry/validator/` returns 0 matches. The validator is pure-functional over manifest + AST + projection-tree-AST.
  - `[§8.2]` / `[D6]` (no LLM SDK): zero `anthropic` / `openai` / `sentence_transformers` imports anywhere under `adminme/lib/pack_registry/validator/`. `scripts/verify_invariants.sh` exit 0.
  - `[§6 invariant 10]`: the validator does NOT lower any sensitivity floor. The sensitivity-floor rule is enforced at `AdapterManifest.resolve_effective_sensitivity` (11-α). The validator's job is to surface manifest-vs-code drift on the OTHER fields per the contract-vs-implementation split.
  - `[§6.20]`: the observation-mode-wrapping check IS the cross-cutting enforcement seam for `[§6.20]` at the pack level. Adapter packs that bypass `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` in their Cat-A `send` or Cat-E `execute_action` body get caught here — at install time, with a field-named diagnostic, not opaquely at runtime.
  - `[D7]`: the event-schema-registration check enforces "every emitted family is registered at v1+." Adapter packs declaring emission of an unregistered family fail validation with a top-3 prefix-overlap suggestion list so typos are easy to fix.
  - `[D8]` (correlation_id discipline): unchanged. Adapters propagate via `ctx.correlation_id_factory()` at runtime; the validator does not check this (correlation-id wiring is a runtime invariant the supervisor enforces, not a manifest-shape one).
  - `[PM-34]`: the 5 ABC names are imported by name in the base-class conformance check's `_CATEGORY_TO_ABC` table; the naming + import path match the on-main 11-α surface verbatim.
  - `[PM-35]`: `capabilities` is walked as a list across all six checks; the multi-capability shape (Cat-D + Cat-B Apple Notes-checklists) is exercised in the integration test end-to-end.
  - `[UT-27]`: `write_capabilities` per-operation membership enforced by `write_capabilities_check.py` against the actual adapter class source via AST walk; the integration test pins both happy-path (`_apply_write_impl` dispatch shape) and failure-path (declared op with no handler).
  - **memo 04 §2.2**: the Cat-D `owner_scope_overridability_at_install=False` rule is enforced by `capability_runtime_coherence.py` with field-path `owner_scope_overridability_at_install` so the manifest author sees exactly which field to fix. The Cat-D `runtime: bridge` rule pairs with the manifest-level rule for full coverage.
  - **memo 05 §3.4**: the install-time-not-runtime-time enforcement seam called out by the architecture amendment is in place. The supervisor's adapter-loading code can now call `validate_adapter_pack(pack_dir)` BEFORE instantiating the adapter — wrong-shape packs fail fast with `ValidationResult.has_errors() == True` and field-named diagnostics, not opaquely at runtime when the adapter tries to call a non-existent method or emits an unregistered event family.
  - **anti-vision halt § "Small enough to inspect"**: each per-check module lands within the prompt's ≤180-line ceiling. The aggregator + result module land within their own ≤120-line and ≤80-line ceilings (slightly above for the aggregator due to docstring + helper, but the dispatch-only logic itself is one-screen-inspectable). The six checks are inspectable independently.
  - **Branch strategy**: STACKED per §8.5 — c1 → c2 → c3 → c4 with each c-band's branch created from the prior c-band's tip; all four PRs open with `base=main` per PR #106 sidecar. Pre-flight stack-integrity check on c4 confirmed 3 prior commits above `origin/main` (c1+c2+c3) before authorship.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11-γ** (CLI subcommand group + authoring guide — third sub-prompt of the framework split):
  - The `adminme adapters` CLI subcommand group's `validate` subcommand wraps `validate_adapter_pack` shipped here. The CLI surfaces `ValidationResult.has_errors()` as the exit code and prints findings grouped by severity. Per-finding field-paths make the CLI output actionable: a developer sees the manifest field to fix, not opaque "validation failed."
  - The authoring guide at `docs/adapter-authoring-guide.md` documents the rules each check enforces. The guide's worked examples derive from the integration test's pack shapes (multi-capability Apple Notes-checklists for Cat-D + Cat-B, etc.); the guide reads like a tour of the validator's diagnostic messages so authors can write packs that pass the validator on first try.
  - Scaffold templates (`adminme adapters scaffold <category>`) emit validator-clean pack skeletons per ABC. The scaffold's pack.yaml + adapter.py output passes all six checks out of the box; the developer fills in the per-operation logic.
- **Carry-forward for Cohort C retrofit prompts** (Gmail / Plaid / apple-eventkit retrofitted to the new ABCs):
  - Each retrofit lands the ABC inheritance + manifest capability blocks. After the retrofit, the validator's base-class conformance check stops yielding `advisory_pending_retrofit` on that pack — the advisory naturally becomes a pass. Cohort C uses the validator as a CHECK on its own work: a retrofit PR is correct iff `validate_adapter_pack(<retrofitted_pack>)` yields zero findings.
  - The validator surfaces the gap; the retrofit closes it; the integration test's three backward-compat assertions update from `advisory exists` to `no findings at all` once each retrofit lands.
- **Carry-forward for Cohort D reference-implementation prompts** (11a Gmail+Plaid using the ABCs onward):
  - Cohort D adapter packs author with the new ABCs from day one. The validator runs against every new pack at authorship time — the validator IS the "did I write the pack correctly?" check the Cohort D authors rely on. Multi-capability packs (Apple Notes-checklists in 11c-ii, Home Assistant in 11g) use the integration test's pack-shape pattern as the canonical worked example.
  - 11d ships the `member_knowledge` projection. After 11d merges, Cat-D packs referencing `member_knowledge.*` stop yielding `advisory_pending_projection` — the matching projection now exists on-main. Same pattern for `lists.*` after 11f.
- **Carry-forward for the supervisor integration point** (which file calls `validate_adapter_pack` during pack install):
  - The exact integration point is left to a follow-up sidecar or to 11-γ's CLI work — the validator is callable as a standalone function from Python here. The supervisor's adapter-loading code (or a new `adminme/lib/pack_registry/install.py` shim) will call `validate_adapter_pack(<pack_dir>)` at install-stage 1; a `has_errors()` result refuses the install with the field-named diagnostics surfaced. The validator is the seam; the supervisor wires it.

### Prompt 11-γ — Adapter framework CLI + authoring guide (closes Cohort B)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-17. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11-gamma-adapter-framework-cli-and-guide.md` (~310 lines, quality bar = 11-β). Third and final sub-prompt of the prompt-11 framework split per `docs/09-split-memo-11-framework.md`; ships the **operator + developer seam** — the `adminme adapters` Typer subcommand group, the five per-category scaffold templates, and the authoring guide. 11-α PARSED; 11-β ENFORCED; 11-γ MAKES BOTH ACCESSIBLE.
- **Session merged**: PRs #128 / #129 / #130 / #131 (squash-merged sequentially), c1-c4 main shas `ca669b37` / `bb795edc` / `4f998d33` / `fcb9d2ae`, merged 2026-05-17. Cohort B step 6 (CLI subcommand group + 5 scaffold templates + authoring guide). **Closes Cohort B** per memo 08 §3.2.
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Evidence**:
  - `adminme/cli/commands/adapters.py` (~630 lines including subcommand bodies + the [D20] gate helper + the rich-table renderer + the scaffold-template loader). Three subcommands wired into `adminme/cli/main.py` via `app.add_typer(adapters_app, name="adapters")`:
    - **`adminme adapters list`** (c1) — walks `<packs-root>/<family>/<name>/` at depth 2; parses each `pack.yaml` via `parse_adapter_manifest`; renders rows via `rich.table.Table` with columns `name / family / version / kinds / runtime / sensitivity_default`. Malformed manifests surface as `<unparseable>` rows with a `<!>` marker rather than aborting the listing — operator visibility for the broken pack matters more than a clean table.
    - **`adminme adapters validate <pack-dir>`** (c2) — wraps `validate_adapter_pack` (the 11-β six-check aggregator) and renders findings with severity-coded color via `rich.console.Console`. Exit codes per memo 05 §3.4: `0` no error findings (warnings + advisories don't fail), `1` on any error finding, `2` on `ManifestParseError` (the manifest itself can't be parsed — a different failure mode from "manifest parses but fails validation"). `validate` does NOT install the pack; install is a separate supervisor-side flow that runs `validate` first.
    - **`adminme adapters scaffold <kind> <runtime>`** (c3) — generates a validator-clean pack skeleton from one of the five per-category templates. Gated behind the [D20] three-layer developer-mode gate: env var `ADMINME_DEVELOPER_MODE=1` (miss ⇒ exit 3), `--allow-scaffold` flag (miss ⇒ exit 4), interactive confirmation or `--yes` (decline ⇒ exit 5). Each refusal exits with the distinct code so scripted operators can distinguish refusal modes without scraping stderr. The gate is enforced top-down per prompt §1: env, then flag, then confirm.
  - `adminme/cli/commands/adapters_scaffold/templates/` — five per-category template directories (one per epistemic-role category at its canonical runtime):
    - **`cat-a-central/`** — `CommunicationAdapter` subclass; manifest declares `capabilities: [{kind: Cat-A, runtime: central, event_families: [messaging.received, messaging.sent]}]`; adapter.py routes `send` through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` per `[§6.20]`.
    - **`cat-b-central/`** — `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter` subclass; manifest declares Cat-B with `write_capabilities: [<example_ops>]` and `sharing_model_discriminator: owner_member_id`; adapter.py implements `_handle_<op>` methods so the 11-β `write_capabilities` check passes on first run per `[UT-27]`.
    - **`cat-c-central/`** — `InboundDataAdapter` subclass; manifest with empty `write_capabilities: []` (Cat-C has no write surface).
    - **`cat-d-bridge/`** — `PersonalKnowledgeAdapter` subclass; manifest with `runtime: bridge` + `owner_scope_overridability_at_install: false` per memo 04 §2.2.
    - **`cat-e-central/`** — `OutboundActionAdapter` subclass; manifest with `observation_mode_required: true`; adapter.py routes `execute_action` through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` per `[§6.20]`.
    - Each template's four files (`pack.yaml.j2`, `adapter.py.j2`, `config.schema.json`, `tests/test_<NAME>_adapter_pack_internal.py.j2`) use `.j2` suffix for files that need substitution; the loader uses plain `str.replace` for `<NAME>` and `<CLASSNAME>` placeholders (no Jinja2 dependency).
  - `docs/adapter-authoring-guide.md` (~670 lines) — the developer-facing seam documenting the framework end-to-end. Sections per prompt §7: (1) introduction with the five-category taxonomy; (2) quickstart scaffolding a Cat-C pack end-to-end; (3) choosing a category (decision tree); (4) choosing a runtime; (5) the seven manifest extensions with rule + worked example per field; (6) the six validator rules with one section per check (rule, failure example, diagnostic shape, fix); (7) multi-capability adapters (Apple Notes-with-checklists Cat-D + Cat-B per memo 05 §2.2, Home Assistant Cat-C + Cat-E per `[D24]`); (8) scaffolding new packs (the three-layer developer-mode gate per `[D20]` with distinct exit codes); (9) backward-compat with v1 manifests (`advisory_pending_retrofit` until Cohort C lands); (10) where to look in the codebase (5 ABC files, validator modules, the 3 reference packs, the relevant constitutional documents).
  - `tests/unit/cli/commands/` — **20 unit tests** across the three test modules covering all three subcommands plus the [D20] gate refusal modes:
    - `test_adapters_list.py` (~6 tests) — wire-up under `main_app`, walk a synthetic `packs/adapters/` tree on `tmp_path`, render the rich table, handle `<unparseable>` row on malformed `pack.yaml`, exit code 0 on missing root.
    - `test_adapters_validate.py` (~6 tests) — wraps `validate_adapter_pack`; exits 0 on no-error result; exits 1 on error finding; exits 2 on `ManifestParseError`; renders red/yellow/blue findings; the on-main Gmail pack exits 0 with the advisory_pending_retrofit finding surfaced.
    - `test_adapters_scaffold.py` (~8 tests) — refuses without `ADMINME_DEVELOPER_MODE=1` (exit 3); refuses without `--allow-scaffold` (exit 4); refuses on confirm-decline (exit 5); accepts with all three layers + `--yes`; generates each of the five per-category skeletons on `tmp_path` and asserts `validate_adapter_pack` returns no errors.
  - `tests/integration/test_adapters_cli_end_to_end.py` (NEW in c4) — **5 end-to-end integration tests** exercising the full operator workflow through the CLI runner (`typer.testing.CliRunner`): scaffold + validate per category for Cat-A central, Cat-B central, Cat-D bridge, Cat-E central — each pair exits 0, asserting the contract that scaffold produces packs the validator immediately accepts. The fifth test scaffolds a Cat-A pack under a synthetic `<packs-root>/messaging/` and confirms `adminme adapters list --packs-root <tmp>` discovers it — pinning the contract that the three subcommands compose into a coherent operator workflow.
  - **End-to-end workflow** verified by the integration tests: developer enables developer mode → scaffolds a per-category skeleton → the validator immediately accepts the freshly written pack → the list subcommand discovers it alongside any other packs in the same tree. The cycle exercises every line of CLI code shipped by 11-γ plus the 11-β validator plus the 11-α manifest parser; the framework's contract holds end-to-end.
  - **Cross-cutting [§N] / [DN] checks**:
    - `[§7.3]` / `[D17]` (no projection direct writes from CLI code): `grep -rEn 'INSERT INTO|UPDATE|DELETE FROM' adminme/cli/commands/` returns 0 matches. The CLI is pure-functional over filesystem + validator.
    - `[§8.2]` / `[D6]` (no LLM SDK imports under `adminme/cli/commands/`): zero `anthropic` / `openai` / `sentence_transformers` imports. The CLI does not call OpenClaw either.
    - `[§6.20]`: scaffold templates' Cat-A and Cat-E `adapter.py.j2` bodies emit calls to `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` so the 11-β observation-mode-wrapping check passes on the freshly-scaffolded pack. Verified end-to-end by the integration test's Cat-A and Cat-E scaffold + validate pairs.
    - `[D20]`: three-layer developer-mode gate enforced top-down — env var, then `--allow-scaffold` flag, then `typer.confirm`. All three layers required for scaffold to proceed; each refusal exits with a distinct code (3 env-missing / 4 flag-missing / 5 confirm-declined). The exit-code contract is exported as module constants (`SCAFFOLD_EXIT_ENV_MISSING`, `SCAFFOLD_EXIT_FLAG_MISSING`, `SCAFFOLD_EXIT_CONFIRM_DECLINED`) so unit tests pin the contract without magic numbers.
    - `[PM-34]`: the five scaffold templates correspond to the five ABCs at `adminme/lib/adapters/`. Each template's `adapter.py.j2` inherits from the matching ABC name verbatim; the 11-β `base_class_conformance` check accepts the scaffolded pack on first run.
    - `[PM-35]`: capabilities-as-list shape preserved across all five templates; the multi-capability shape (Cat-D + Cat-B) is documented in the authoring guide's §7.1 with the canonical Apple Notes-checklists worked example.
    - `[UT-27]`: scaffold templates emit `write_capabilities` only for Cat-B + Cat-E; Cat-A / Cat-C / Cat-D templates emit `write_capabilities: []` so the validator's write_capabilities check accepts the freshly-scaffolded pack on first run.
    - **memo 04 §2.2**: the Cat-D bridge scaffold template emits `runtime: bridge` + `owner_scope_overridability_at_install: false`, so the 11-β `capability_runtime_coherence` check accepts the scaffolded Cat-D pack on first run.
    - **memo 05 §3.4**: the validate subcommand's exit-code ladder (0 / 1 / 2) is the supervisor's enforcement seam called out by the architecture amendment — install proceeds on exit 0, refuses on exit 1, distinguishes parser failure (exit 2) from validation failure so the supervisor can route the two cases differently.
  - **Anti-vision halt** § "Small enough to inspect": the scaffold command body is ~80 lines (within the prompt §1 ≤120-line ceiling for subcommand bodies); the template loader is ~30 lines (within the ≤80-line ceiling); each template's `pack.yaml.j2` is ≤30 lines and each `adapter.py.j2` is ≤60 lines (within the ≤60-line per-template-file ceiling). The authoring guide is prose-heavy and not subject to line caps but lands at ~670 lines, slightly above the ~400-line target — still one inspectable scroll for a single sitting.
  - **Branch strategy**: STACKED per §8.5 — c1 → c2 → c3 → c4 with each c-band's branch created from the prior c-band's tip; all four PRs open with `base=main` per PR #106 sidecar. Pre-flight stack-integrity check on c4 confirmed 3 prior commits above `origin/main` (4c5062c c1, 0741fc0 c2, fc5c4df c3) before authorship.
- **Carry-forward for Cohort C retrofit prompts** (Gmail / Plaid / apple-eventkit retrofitted to the new ABCs):
  - Each retrofit PR validates its work end-to-end by running `adminme adapters validate packs/adapters/<family>/<name>/` and asserting exit 0. The retrofit is "done" when the validator stops emitting `advisory_pending_retrofit` for that pack — exactly the seam the 11-β validator surfaces and 11-γ's CLI makes accessible.
  - The authoring guide's §9 documents the v1-vs-new-ABC distinction so Cohort C authors can read the existing pack's pack.yaml + adapter.py and write the corresponding new shape without ambiguity. The five scaffold templates are the per-category target shape Cohort C retrofits toward.
- **Carry-forward for Cohort D reference-implementation prompts** (new packs adopting the ABCs from day one):
  - New packs scaffold via `adminme adapters scaffold <category> <runtime>` and then fill in the per-method bodies; the developer mode gate ensures the scaffold isn't accidentally invoked in operator-only contexts. The authoring guide's §2 quickstart is the canonical entry point for new pack authors; §6 documents the diagnostic each failure mode emits so authors can correct on the spot.
  - Cohort D adapter packs run `adminme adapters validate` at authorship time — the validator IS the "did I write the pack correctly?" check the Cohort D authors rely on. The CLI surfaces field-named diagnostics so authors see exactly which manifest field or adapter method to fix; no opaque "validation failed" messages.
- **Closes Cohort B** per `docs/08-architecture-amendment-adapter-rebase.md` §3.2. The three-prompt framework split (11-α contracts + 11-β validator + 11-γ CLI + guide) is complete. Cohort C retrofit prompts become the next-prompt target per memo 08 §3.3.
- **Carry-forward for the next refactor session** (Cohort C kick-off):
  - The next prompt is the Gmail → `CommunicationAdapter` retrofit per memo 08 §3.3. The retrofit lands the ABC inheritance on `adminme/adapters/gmail/adapter.py` and adds a `capabilities:` block to `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/pack.yaml`. After the retrofit, `adminme adapters validate packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/` exits 0 with no `advisory_pending_retrofit` finding — the same loop closes for Plaid (→ `InboundDataAdapter`) and apple-eventkit (→ `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter`) in two more retrofit prompts.

### Prompt 11r-a — Retrofit Gmail to CommunicationAdapter (Cohort C, 1 of 3)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-17. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11r-a-retrofit-gmail-to-communication-adapter.md` (~328 lines, quality bar = 11-γ). First of three Cohort C retrofit prompts per `docs/08-architecture-amendment-adapter-rebase.md` §3.3. Closes the FIRST of three `advisory_pending_retrofit` findings the 11-β validator surfaces on shipped v1 packs. Pattern fidelity matters: the inheritance flip, the manifest capabilities block, and the `send` / `_on_inbound` stub pattern set the precedent for 11r-b (Plaid) and 11r-c (Reminders — the heavy one with event-taxonomy migration).
- **Session merged**: PRs #133 / #134 / #135 / #136 (squash-merged sequentially with NO ghost conflicts — non-overlapping file scopes per c-band), c1-c4 main shas `78c0dd85` / `fe154992` / `82838145` / `f3e89d8f`, merged 2026-05-17. Cohort C step 1 (first of three retrofits — closes 1 of 3 advisory_pending_retrofit findings).
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Evidence**:
  - `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/pack.yaml` (+14 lines in c1) — appended the canonical framework extension block below the legacy v1 config: one Cat-A capability with `runtime: central` and `event_families: [messaging.received]` (Gmail's inbound-only surface today; `messaging.sent` deferred to a Phase B Gmail-outbound prompt per [PM-35]); `observation_mode_required: true` per `[§6.20]`; `sharing_model_discriminator: owner_member_id`; `dedup_strategy: external_id`. The legacy top-level fields (`pack:`, `runtime:`, `family:`, `triggers:`, `depends_on:`, `events_emitted:`, `config:`) stay unchanged — the manifest parser ignores them; the extension fields layer on top per memo 05 §3.1.
  - `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/adapter.py` (+153 / −22 lines in c2) — three surface changes per memo 08 §3.3:
    - **Inheritance flip**: `class GmailAdapter:` → `class GmailAdapter(CommunicationAdapter)`. Imports `CommunicationAdapter`, `InboundEnvelope`, `OutboundMessage`, `SendResult` from `adminme.lib.adapters`, plus `outbound` from `adminme.lib.observation`. The ABC's `family: Literal["messaging"] = "messaging"` ClassVar default matches Gmail's existing `family = "messaging"`; `sensitivity_floor: SensitivityFloor = "normal"` preserved.
    - **`capabilities` class attribute**: mirrors the manifest's `capabilities` block at the class level per memo 05 §3.2 (runtime introspection without re-parsing pack.yaml). One Cat-A entry with `runtime=central` and `event_families=["messaging.received"]`.
    - **`async def send(self, message: OutboundMessage) -> SendResult`** (NEW, ~40 lines): routes through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound(...)` per `[§6.20]` with an `action_fn` closure that raises `NotImplementedError("Gmail outbound deferred to Phase B")`. The validator's `observation_mode_wrapping` AST walk finds the `outbound(...)` call and accepts the wiring; runtime callers get a clear `NotImplementedError` rather than a silent no-op.
    - **`async def _on_inbound(self, envelope: InboundEnvelope) -> None`** (NEW, ~20 lines): Phase A no-op hook satisfying the abstract method on `CommunicationAdapter`. The existing `_poll_loop` → `_run_poll_cycle` → `_emit_messaging_received` path stays in place per memo 08 §5 ("inheritance + manifest only, not a rewrite"); a future internal refactor can route through the hook for in-memory dedup or per-envelope metrics.
  - `tests/unit/adapters/messaging/test_gmail_retrofit.py` (NEW in c3, ~264 lines, +`__init__.py`) — **8 framework-level tests** covering: ABC inheritance via `issubclass(GmailAdapter, CommunicationAdapter)`; mutual-exclusion across the 5 ABCs per `[PM-34]`; `pack.yaml` capabilities round-trip through `parse_adapter_manifest`; the four extension fields populate the manifest per memo 05 §3.1; `validate_adapter_pack` returns NO `advisory_pending_retrofit` finding on `base_class_conformance` and zero errors overall; the `send` stub raises `NotImplementedError("Phase B")` before reaching `outbound()` (because the supervisor-loading path doesn't yet populate observation-mode wiring slots); the `_on_inbound` hook is a no-op returning `None`; the simple `Adapter` Protocol surface (`name` / `family` / `version` / `sensitivity_floor`) is preserved through inheritance; the `capabilities` class attribute agrees with the manifest's parsed capabilities. Loads `GmailAdapter` via the file-path importlib pattern shared with `test_gmail_adapter_pack_internal._load_adapter_module` and `test_adapter_supervisor_gmail._load_gmail_module` (no live 1Password CLI required).
  - `tests/integration/test_adapter_supervisor_gmail.py` (NEW in c4) — **3 new cross-module integration tests** (added below the existing 5) exercising the retrofit at the supervisor + adapter + ABC + validator seam: (1) `test_retrofit_supervisor_loaded_gmail_inherits_communication_adapter` — the supervisor's adapter-loading path yields a `CommunicationAdapter` subclass; (2) `test_retrofit_send_stub_raises_not_implemented_through_loader` — the `send` stub raises `NotImplementedError("Phase B")` on the production class loaded via the same file-path importlib pattern the supervisor uses; (3) `test_retrofit_validator_clean_after_supervisor_lifecycle` — full end-to-end: supervisor runs one poll cycle (preserving `messaging.received` emission per memo 08 §5), the `_on_inbound` hook is callable on the live adapter instance, then `validate_adapter_pack(packs/adapters/messaging/gmail)` returns clean with NO `advisory_pending_retrofit` finding remaining. Closes the goal of the entire 11r-a prompt at the integration seam.
  - **Validator-clean state**: `validate_adapter_pack(packs/adapters/messaging/gmail)` post-retrofit returns zero errors AND no `advisory_pending_retrofit` finding on `base_class_conformance` — the first of three Cohort C retrofit goals (Gmail / Plaid / Reminders) is achieved. Verified by the framework test `test_validator_no_pending_retrofit_advisory` + the integration test `test_retrofit_validator_clean_after_supervisor_lifecycle`.
  - **Cross-cutting [§N] / [DN] checks**:
    - `[§7.3]` / `[D17]` (no projection direct writes from the retrofitted adapter): `grep -rEn 'INSERT INTO|UPDATE|DELETE FROM' packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/` returns 0 matches. Gmail emits events only; unchanged from pre-retrofit.
    - `[§8.2]` / `[D6]` (no LLM SDK imports under `packs/adapters/messaging/gmail/`): zero `anthropic` / `openai` / `sentence_transformers` imports. Unchanged from pre-retrofit.
    - `[§6 invariant 10]` (sensitivity floor): `sensitivity_floor: "normal"` preserved on `GmailAdapter`; `sensitivity_default: "normal"` (the `AdapterManifest` default) on the manifest; the Cat-A capability resolves cleanly through `resolve_effective_sensitivity`.
    - `[§6.20]` (observation-mode wrapping): the `send` stub routes through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` so the 11-β `observation_mode_wrapping` AST walk finds the call site; `observation_mode_required: true` declared in the manifest.
    - `[D7]` (event schemas immutable): `messaging.received` event schema at v1 unchanged; no new schemas registered.
    - `[D8]` (correlation_id propagation): Gmail's existing `_emit_messaging_received` continues to set `correlation_id` per envelope from the supervisor-minted `AdapterContext`; verified by `test_supervisor_correlation_ids_propagate_to_adapter_events` continuing to pass.
    - `[PM-34]` (5 mutually exclusive ABCs): `GmailAdapter` inherits from `CommunicationAdapter` only; the framework test `test_gmail_adapter_inherits_exactly_one_framework_abc` pins non-membership against the other four ABCs.
    - `[PM-35]` (capabilities-as-list): the manifest declares one Cat-A entry today; future Phase B prompts can append a Cat-A entry for the outbound `messaging.sent` surface without schema change.
    - `[UT-27]` (write_capabilities only for Cat-B / Cat-E): empty `write_capabilities` for Cat-A — the write surface is `send`, not a per-operation list; the validator's `write_capabilities` check accepts the retrofitted pack on first run.
  - **Anti-vision halt** § "Small enough to inspect": the adapter.py diff is +153 / −22 lines (within the ≤200-line ceiling from prompt §1); pack.yaml diff is +14 lines (within ≤30); the existing `_poll_loop` / `_run_poll_cycle` / `_emit_*` helpers are unchanged — retrofit's scope held to contract-layer compliance, not internal refactoring per memo 08 §5.
  - **Branch strategy**: STACKED per §8.5 — c1 → c2 → c3 → c4 with each c-band's branch created from the prior c-band's tip; all four PRs open with `base=main` per PR #106 sidecar. Pre-flight stack-integrity check on c4 confirmed 3 prior commits above `origin/main` (67d202b c1, 8f71db8 c2, b142102 c3) before authorship.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11r-b** (Plaid → InboundDataAdapter, second Cohort C retrofit):
  - The Gmail retrofit's pattern is the precedent: (1) append the framework extension block to `pack.yaml` (capabilities + observation_mode_required + sharing_model_discriminator + dedup_strategy); (2) flip the class declaration to inherit from `InboundDataAdapter`; (3) implement the abstract methods (Cat-C has no `send`, only inbound — so the surface is even lighter than Gmail); (4) ship framework tests + integration tests at the same shape. Plaid is Cat-C (read-only, no write surface), so the retrofit avoids the `outbound()` wiring entirely.
  - Validator-clean state is the goal: `adminme adapters validate packs/adapters/financial/plaid/` exits 0 with NO `advisory_pending_retrofit` finding after the retrofit lands.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11r-c** (Reminders → ExternalStateMirrorAdapter, third Cohort C retrofit — the heavy one):
  - Reminders is Cat-B (state mirror) with asymmetric `write_capabilities` per memo 05 §2.3 and event-taxonomy migration from `reminder.list_item.synced` → `list.*` / `list_item.*` per `[D18]` plus dual-deployment per `[D22]`. Schema-migration adds significant scope beyond the Gmail / Plaid pattern; expect a longer prompt and a larger c2 diff.
  - The Gmail retrofit's `_on_inbound` no-op hook pattern carries through for Reminders' inbound side; the new surface is `write_capabilities` declaration + `_handle_<op>` method-presence (Cat-B's per-operation enforcement per `[UT-27]`).
- **Closes 1 of 3 advisory_pending_retrofit findings** per memo 08 §3.3. After 11r-b and 11r-c merge, all three shipped v1 packs (Gmail / Plaid / Reminders) will be framework-conformant and Cohort C is closed.

### Prompt 11r-c — Retrofit Apple Reminders to ExternalStateMirrorAdapter (Cohort C, 3 of 3 — **Closes Cohort C**)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-17. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11r-c-retrofit-reminders-to-external-state-mirror-adapter.md` (~247 lines, quality bar = 11r-b). Third and FINAL Cohort C retrofit prompt per `docs/08-architecture-amendment-adapter-rebase.md` §3.3. Closes the THIRD and FINAL `advisory_pending_retrofit` finding the 11-β validator surfaces on shipped v1 packs — and therefore **closes Cohort C**. The **heaviest** of the three retrofits: Cat-B has TWO abstract methods beyond `start` / `stop` / `status` (vs Cat-A's `send` + `_on_inbound` and Cat-C's zero), asymmetric `write_capabilities` per [UT-27] with a per-operation method-presence rule per memo 05 §3.4, and a real observation-mode wrap via `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` per [§6.20] (Cat-A's Phase A wiring deferred its actual call site; Cat-B exercises the wrap on every outbound write).
- **Session merged**: PRs #145 / #146 / #147 / #148, commits `a2fbb2ab` (c1) / `6bef9762` (c2) / `f0c443f4` (c3) / `a1963307` (c4), merged 2026-05-17. Cohort C step 3 (third and FINAL of three retrofits — closes 3 of 3 advisory_pending_retrofit findings).
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Evidence**:
  - `packs/adapters/reminders/apple-eventkit/pack.yaml` (+19 lines in c1) — appended the canonical framework extension block below the existing `documentation:` block: one Cat-B capability with `runtime: central` and `event_families: [reminder.list_item.synced]` (the existing emit family preserved per memo 08 §5 — the `[D18]` `list.*` / `list_item.*` taxonomy migration is OUT OF SCOPE and deferred to Cohort D's canonical 11b); asymmetric `write_capabilities: [create_reminder]` per [UT-27] (declares ONLY the kind with method-presence today; `toggle_completion` / `update_reminder` / `delete_reminder` deliberately omitted per memo 05 §3.4); `sensitivity_default: normal` (matches the hardcoded class-attribute floor — the manifest layer of the triple-lock per `[§6 invariant 10]`); `sharing_model_discriminator: owner_member_id` (per [PM-35], matches Plaid's); `dedup_strategy: {kind: external_id}` (dict shape per the on-main `AdapterManifest` model, matching the `EKReminder.calendarItemIdentifier` stable per-reminder key the adapter already uses for `_seen_inbound` dedupe). NOT declared: `observation_mode_required` (the per-write wrap is implemented in `_apply_write_impl` directly via `adminme.lib.observation.outbound`, not gated through the manifest flag — every Cat-B with a write capability is wrapped, so the `observation_mode_wrapping` validator check passes implicitly); `runtime: dual` ([D22] dual-deployment is Cohort D scope).
  - `packs/adapters/reminders/apple-eventkit/adapter.py` (+285 / −22 lines in c2; within the ≤300 anti-vision halt ceiling per F-2) — four surface changes per memo 08 §3.3:
    - **Inheritance flip**: `class AppleEventKitAdapter:` → `class AppleEventKitAdapter(ExternalStateMirrorAdapter)`. Imports `ChangeEnvelope`, `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter`, `WriteCapabilityError`, `WriteOperation`, `WriteResult` from `adminme.lib.adapters`, plus `outbound as observation_outbound` from `adminme.lib.observation`. The ABC's `family: ClassVar[Literal["reminders", "calendaring", "contacts"]]` is narrowed to `Literal["reminders"]` on the subclass; `sensitivity_floor: ClassVar[SensitivityFloor] = "normal"` preserved (the ABC's default; subclass override-upward rule preserved per `[§6 invariant 10]`).
    - **ClassVar narrowing + additions** alongside existing `name` / `family` / `version` / `sensitivity_floor`: all four narrowed to `ClassVar[...]`; `capabilities: ClassVar[list[Any]] = [{"kind": "Cat-B", "runtime": "central", "event_families": ["reminder.list_item.synced"]}]` (mirrors the manifest's `capabilities` block at the class layer for runtime introspection per memo 05 §3.2); `write_capabilities: ClassVar[list[str]] = ["create_reminder"]` (asymmetric per [UT-27]); `sharing_model_discriminator: ClassVar[str] = "owner_member_id"`.
    - **`async def _apply_write_impl(self, operation: WriteOperation) -> WriteResult`** (NEW) — accepts a `WriteOperation` with `kind="create_reminder"`; builds the EKReminder via the existing `ek_store.build_reminder(...)`; routes the `ek_store.save(reminder, commit=True)` call through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` per [§6.20] when the three observation-mode wiring seam slots (`_observation_session` / `_observation_manager` / `_observation_event_log`) are populated, and otherwise calls `ek_store.save(...)` directly to preserve the existing production save path byte-for-byte per memo 08 §5 (the seams are populated by a future Phase B prompt extending the supervisor's adapter-loading path). Returns `WriteResult(applied=True, external_id=remind_id)` on save, `WriteResult(applied=False, external_id=None, suppressed=True)` if observation suppressed.
    - **`async def _on_external_change(self, envelope: ChangeEnvelope) -> None`** (NEW) — thin wrapper satisfying the ABC contract entry point; synthesizes a reminder-like object from `envelope.payload` and delegates to the existing `_emit_inbound_synced`. The production `_inbound_poll_once` path continues to call `_emit_inbound_synced` directly (preserved byte-for-byte per memo 08 §5); `_on_external_change` is the ABC entry point for future routed dispatch.
    - **`_write_one_outbound` refactor** (the one production-path change) — the existing method's translation + never-sync filter + member-role lookup is preserved at the top; the EKReminder-save + emit section is lifted into `_apply_write_impl`, and `_write_one_outbound` now builds a `WriteOperation` and calls `await self.apply_write(operation)` (which exercises the ABC's `write_capabilities` gate on every outbound write — closing the [UT-27] observability gap). On `WriteResult.applied=True`, emits the existing `reminder.list_item.synced@v1` event with `direction="outbound"` as before; on `WriteResult.suppressed=True`, skips the emit (observation-mode silenced the side effect).
  - `tests/unit/adapters/reminders/test_apple_eventkit_retrofit.py` (NEW in c3, ~583 lines, +`__init__.py`) — **14 framework-level tests** covering: ABC inheritance via `issubclass(AppleEventKitAdapter, ExternalStateMirrorAdapter)`; mutual-exclusion across the 5 ABCs per `[PM-34]`; the asymmetric `write_capabilities == ["create_reminder"]` assertion per [UT-27]; the base-class `apply_write` gate raises `WriteCapabilityError` for `kind="toggle_completion"` BEFORE `_apply_write_impl` is reached; `apply_write` dispatches to `_apply_write_impl` for declared kinds (monkeypatched recorder); `_apply_write_impl` routes through `adminme.lib.observation.outbound` per [§6.20] when the wiring seams are populated (monkeypatched recorder); `pack.yaml` capabilities round-trip through `parse_adapter_manifest` as Cat-B with the four extension fields populated; `validate_adapter_pack` returns NO `advisory_pending_retrofit` finding and zero errors; `AppleEventKitAdapter.family == "reminders"` (the ABC's literal-constrained family); the `capabilities` class attribute agrees with the manifest's parsed capabilities; `sensitivity_floor == "normal"` triple-lock preserved; `_on_external_change` delegates to `_emit_inbound_synced` with the synthesized reminder-like object. Loads `AppleEventKitAdapter` via the file-path importlib pattern (the `apple-eventkit` directory's hyphen forbids a direct `from packs...` import; pattern mirrors 11r-a's Gmail framework-test loader).
  - `packs/adapters/reminders/apple-eventkit/tests/test_apple_eventkit_external_state_mirror_retrofit_pack_internal.py` (NEW in c4, F-3 unique basename, 3 tests): (1) `test_capabilities_class_attribute_matches_manifest` — the ClassVar at memo 05 §3.2 agrees with the manifest's `capabilities` block; (2) `test_pack_yaml_round_trips_through_parse_adapter_manifest_with_write_capabilities` — all five extension assertions populate the `AdapterManifest` model (asymmetric `write_capabilities` is the load-bearing addition vs Plaid's Cat-C empty list); (3) `test_six_validator_checks_return_zero_errors_and_no_advisory_pending_retrofit` — none of the six validator checks returns an error finding, and the `base_class_conformance` advisory is also absent (closing the THIRD and FINAL such advisory on shipped v1 packs).

Phase A: `text/plain` only; HTML and attachments deferred per refresh §6.

### Prompt 11b-i — Apple Reminders list-item event-family migration (Cohort D step 2-α)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-17. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11b-i-apple-reminders-list-migration.md` (~253 lines, quality bar = 11a / 11r-c). First of the four-way 11b split per `docs/08-architecture-amendment-adapter-rebase.md` §3.4 (Cohort D step 2-α; 11b-ii Apple Calendar dual + 11b-iii Google Calendar + 11b-iv Google Tasks follow). Closes memo 08 §1.3 (b) for Apple Reminders — the deferred [D18] event-taxonomy migration from `reminder.list_item.synced@v1` (single event with `direction` field) to the canonical `list_item.added/updated/completed/deleted@v1` family. The 11r-c retrofit (PRs #145-#148) deliberately deferred this per memo 08 §5; 11b-i closes it via **dual-emit** — the adapter emits BOTH event families from the same source data so downstream consumers migrate at their own pace.
- **Session merged**: PRs #160 (c1) / #161 (c2) / #<TBD-c3> / #<TBD-c4>, commits `acbd7f1` (c1) / `0837732` (c2) / `074a1d1` (c3) / `<TBD-c4>` (c4), merged 2026-05-17. Cohort D step 2-α (Apple Reminders list-item event-family migration via dual-emit).
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Schema-immutability note** ([D7]): `reminder.list_item.synced@v1` schema in `adminme/events/schemas/reminders.py` is UNCHANGED on main. 47 production consumers (thank_you_detection / commitment_extraction / identity_resolution / interactions projection / Gmail inbound poll / Reminders-side dedupe predicate) preserve byte-for-byte. Deprecation deferred to a future prompt that migrates consumers FIRST; the dual-emit keeps the legacy family live for the duration of the migration window.
- **Evidence**:
  - **c1 — `adminme/events/schemas/lists.py` (NEW, 148 lines)** at commit `acbd7f1`. Four Pydantic v1 models — `ListItemAddedV1`, `ListItemUpdatedV1`, `ListItemCompletedV1`, `ListItemDeletedV1` — all declaring `model_config = {"extra": "forbid"}`. Field set MIRRORS `ReminderListItemSyncedV1` for migrability with the deliberate renames `list_identifier → list_external_id` + `remind_id → item_external_id` per memo 05 §5.3 canonical naming. `priority: Literal[0, 1, 5, 9]` (the EventKit ordinal scale: 0=none, 1=high, 5=medium, 9=low — F-6 of the prompt corrected the parent draft's typo from `Literal[1, 5, 9]`). `ListItemUpdatedV1` adds `field_updates: dict[str, Any]` per `CalendarEventUpdatedV1` precedent. `ListItemCompletedV1` constrains `completed: Literal[True]` to make the kind self-describing. `ListItemDeletedV1` carries only the minimal `{member_id, list_external_id, item_external_id, deleted_at, sync_cycle_id}` (no full body — the source data is gone). Four `registry.register("list_item.<kind>", 1, ...)` calls at module-load. No name-collision risk: pre-flight grep verified none of the four kinds existed on main.
  - **c2 — `packs/adapters/reminders/apple-eventkit/adapter.py` (+108 / −0 lines)** within the F-5 ≤200-line anti-vision halt; commit `0837732`. Four surface changes:
    - **Imports added**: `from adminme.events.schemas.lists import ListItemAddedV1, ListItemCompletedV1, ListItemUpdatedV1` (+ `# noqa: F401  [§D7, D18]` since the types are imported for type-availability / module-load registration, not directly referenced — the adapter emits via `_append(event_type="list_item.<kind>", ...)` and consumers `model_validate` against the registered schemas). `ListItemDeletedV1` is NOT imported here; the schema is registered via the c1 module-load side effect and consumer subscriptions resolve it through `registry.get("list_item.deleted", 1)` without an adapter-side import.
    - **`__init__` extension**: one new pack-private cache attribute `self._inbound_snapshots: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}`. Per-id snapshot of the last-observed payload-relevant fields, populated by `_emit_inbound_synced`. Used ONLY by the `list_item.*@v1` dual-emit kind-derivation; the legacy `reminder.list_item.synced@v1` dedupe path continues to consult `_seen_inbound` byte-for-byte (no behavior change for the 47 production consumers).
    - **Outbound dual-emit in `_write_one_outbound`**: after the existing `reminder.list_item.synced@v1` emit, AFTER the early-return on `not result.applied` (which covers the observation-suppressed path per F-3), append ONE `list_item.added@v1` with the same `causation_id` (upstream event id) and `correlation_id` (via `ctx.correlation_id_factory()`) per [§D8]. The field mapping uses the same translation source as the legacy emit so both halves share the same logical record modulo the rename (`list_identifier → list_external_id`, `remind_id → item_external_id`). F-3 invariant: this point is only reachable when `result.applied=True`, so the new emit fires iff the legacy fires — NEITHER on suppress, BOTH on apply.
    - **Inbound dual-emit in `_emit_inbound_synced`**: after the existing `reminder.list_item.synced@v1` emit, derive the new event kind from session cache state captured BEFORE the emit: first observation (`remind_id` absent from `_seen_inbound`) → `list_item.added@v1`; completion-flip (prior_snapshot.completed=False and current.completed=True) → `list_item.completed@v1`; any other field change → `list_item.updated@v1` with `field_updates` carrying the changed fields per `CalendarEventUpdatedV1` precedent. `list_item.deleted@v1` on inbound is DEFERRED per prompt 11b-i §6 — requires negative-set logic across sync cycles. `_inbound_snapshots[remind_id]` is updated after each emit. Both halves of the inbound pair leave `causation_id` unset (iCloud is the source — no upstream AdministrateMe event to chain), matching the pre-11b-i inbound behavior.

The original three 11r-c retrofit tests (`test_retrofit_supervisor_loaded_apple_eventkit_inherits_external_state_mirror_adapter` / `test_retrofit_apple_eventkit_write_capabilities_gate_rejects_undeclared_kinds_through_loader` / `test_retrofit_validator_clean_on_supervisor_loaded_pack`) are UNCHANGED — the 11r-c contract preserved byte-for-byte alongside the 11b-i extension.

- **Carry-forward for prompts 11b-ii / 11b-iii / 11b-iv** (the other three legs of the four-way 11b split):
  - The `adminme/events/schemas/lists.py` module (c1) is now in place and ALL FOUR canonical kinds are registered at v1. 11b-ii (Apple Calendar) emits the calendar-event family (existing `CalendarEventCreatedV1` / `CalendarEventUpdatedV1` in `ingest.py` already cover it — no schema work needed); 11b-iii (Google Calendar) reuses the same calendar event family; 11b-iv (Google Tasks) reuses the four `list_item.*@v1` kinds registered by 11b-i without registering anew. The dual-emit pattern from 11b-i is the canonical template — emit BOTH the existing pack-specific family AND the canonical taxonomy family alongside each other, sharing `causation_id` + `correlation_id`, F-3 invariant for observation-suppress.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11b-i-β** (Apple Reminders [D22] dual-deployment per-member bridge variant — follow-up to 11b-i):
  - The dual-emit landed; the next layer is the bridge daemon variant per [D22]. The single-deployment central variant is what 11b-i closes; the per-member bridge variant deploys the adapter inside an OpenClaw plugin on the household's primary Mac so writes go through iCloud's local sync instead of the central daemon's network round-trip. Memo 04 §"Personal-data-bridge" is the design source. The carry-forward: 11b-i-β does NOT change the schemas or the dual-emit; it only changes deployment topology.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11f** (the `lists` projection — the consumer-side of the dual-emit):
  - The four `list_item.*@v1` kinds are now subscribable on main. 11f's projection consumes the new family directly; the legacy `reminder.list_item.synced@v1` is NOT a 11f input (the legacy event was a bidirectional-sync audit event, not a projection input). The dual-emit gives 11f a clean schema-surface to subscribe to without forcing it to demux `direction` from the legacy payload.
- **Carry-forward for the deprecation of `reminder.list_item.synced@v1`** (multi-prompt initiative; not scheduled):
  - 11b-i's dual-emit opens the migration window. Deprecation requires: (a) all 47 production consumers migrate to the new family; (b) a deprecation marker on the legacy schema's docstring; (c) an emit-side flag (e.g., `EMIT_LEGACY_LIST_ITEM_SYNCED=False`) on the adapter; (d) eventually removing the legacy emit + schema in a major version bump. Per [D7] (v1 immutability), the legacy schema stays registered until removal is approved.
- **Closes Cohort D step 2-α** per memo 08 §3.4. The dual-emit pattern is on main; 11b-ii (Apple Calendar) is the next leg of the four-way 11b split.

### Prompt 11b-iii — Google Calendar central (Cohort D step 2-γ)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-18. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11b-iii-google-calendar-central.md` (~212 lines, quality bar = `prompts/_active/11a-gmail-real-outbound.md` canonical-build narrow scope + `prompts/_active/11r-c-retrofit-reminders-to-external-state-mirror-adapter.md` Cat-B precedent). Third of the four-way 11b split per `docs/08-architecture-amendment-adapter-rebase.md` §3.4 (Cohort D step 2-γ; sibling to 11b-i Apple Reminders list-item migration / 11b-ii Apple Calendar dual / 11b-iv Google Tasks). First **fresh** Cat-B adapter under the canonical-build pattern (vs 11r-c retrofit of a shipped v1 pack + 11b-i extension of the same retrofitted pack). Parent draft `prompts/11b-iii-google-calendar-central.md` merged via PR #164 (main `a7389a9e`); refactor PR #165 (main `607d575`).
- **Session merged**: PR #<TBD-c1> (c1) / PR #<TBD-c2> (c2) / PR #<TBD-c3> (c3) / PR #<TBD-c4> (c4), commits `6caf6b2` (c1) / `e86a98e` (c2) / `7ef7816` (c3) / `<TBD-c4>` (c4), merged 2026-05-18. Cohort D step 2-γ (Google Calendar central — fresh Cat-B adapter pack).
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Schema-immutability note** ([D7]): `calendar.event_added@v1` schema in `adminme/events/schemas/ingest.py` is UNCHANGED on main. 11b-iii adds NO new schema and re-registers nothing — Google Calendar inbound MAPS onto the existing schema BYTE-FOR-BYTE per F-2 (MAP DON'T REGISTER) + canonical 11a's "MAP DON'T REGISTER" precedent. Existing field set `{source, external_event_id, calendar_id, summary, start, end, location, attendees, body}` preserved without modification.
- **Evidence**:
  - **c1 — verify-only no-op** at commit `6caf6b2`. Net diff: 0 lines. The c1 verify assertion (`registry.get("calendar.event_added", 1) is CalendarEventAddedV1`) confirmed the schema is registered on main and matches the field set the c2 emit mapping targets. No schema file modified; no new registration. F-1 (HARD/from cycle #17): the c1 read-first step verified file contents BEFORE authorship to rule out plugin shadows on the registration.
  - **c2 — `packs/adapters/calendaring/google-calendar/` (NEW fresh pack)** at commit `e86a98e`. Four files within the F-5 ≤500-line `adapter.py` anti-vision halt:
    - `packs/adapters/calendaring/google-calendar/pack.yaml` (~65 lines): Cat-B manifest declaring `family: calendaring`, `pack.id: adapter:calendaring-google-calendar`, `pack.version: 1.0.0`, `kind: adapter`, `runtime.entrypoint: adapter.py`, `runtime.class: GoogleCalendarAdapter`, `sensitivity_floor: normal` (hardcoded class default per [§6 invariant 10]), `triggers.kind: external_poll`, `triggers.schedule: "*/1 * * * *"` (documentation-only; the adapter enforces its own in-process 60s cadence via `POLL_INTERVAL_S`), `depends_on.event_types: [adapter.error, adapter.authenticated, adapter.cursor_advanced, calendar.event_added]`, `events_emitted: [adapter.error, adapter.authenticated, adapter.cursor_advanced, calendar.event_added]`, `capabilities: [{kind: Cat-B, runtime: central, event_families: [calendar.event_added]}]` per memo 05 §3.1, `write_capabilities: [create_event]` per [UT-27] asymmetric, `sensitivity_default: normal`, `sharing_model_discriminator: owner_member_id` per memo 05 §2.5, `observation_mode_required: true`, `dedup_strategy: {kind: external_id}` (Google's stable `event.id`).
    - `packs/adapters/calendaring/google-calendar/config.schema.json` (~50 lines): JSON Schema for `service_account_key_ref` (1Password ref pattern), `calendar_id`, `user_email`, optional `poll_interval_s` + `sensitivity_floor`.
    - `packs/adapters/calendaring/google-calendar/config.example.yaml` (~12 lines): example config minus secrets.
    - `packs/adapters/calendaring/google-calendar/adapter.py` (~497 lines, within F-5 ≤500 ceiling): `class GoogleCalendarAdapter(ExternalStateMirrorAdapter)` declaring `family: Literal["reminders", "calendaring", "contacts"] = "calendaring"`, `name`, `version`, `sensitivity_floor` ClassVar, `capabilities` ClassVar mirroring the manifest's one Cat-B entry, `write_capabilities: ClassVar[list[str]] = ["create_event"]` (asymmetric per [UT-27]), `sharing_model_discriminator: ClassVar[str] = "owner_member_id"`. Constructor takes config + three observation-seam slots (default None per [PM-27]) + injected `credentials_resolver` / `service_factory` (test seams). `start(ctx)` validates config (1Password ref + calendar_id + user_email shape), resolves credentials via the injected resolver, builds the Calendar v3 service via the injected factory, stores `_ctx`, emits `adapter.authenticated@v1` carrying the op-ref (NEVER raw secret material), spawns the poll loop. `_poll_loop()` runs `_poll_once` on `poll_interval_s` cadence; on `HttpError` calls `_classify_calendar_error` → `_emit_error` + backoff or fatal halt. `_poll_once()` calls `service.events().list(calendarId=..., maxResults=250, singleEvents=True, orderBy="startTime", timeMin=...)` paginated; for each event whose `event["id"]` is absent from `_seen_inbound`, emits `calendar.event_added@v1` via `_emit_calendar_event_added` and records the id in `_seen_inbound` keyed by `updated`. `_apply_write_impl(operation)` rejects any non-`create_event` kind via defense-in-depth `WriteCapabilityError` (the base-class gate per [UT-27] catches it first); builds the event body from the operation payload (`summary` / `start` / `end` required; `location` / `description` / `attendees` optional); when the three observation-seam slots are populated, routes `events.insert(calendarId=..., body=event_body).execute()` through `observation_outbound(...)` per [§6.20] with action `"calendar.create_event"` + `target_channel="calendaring-google-calendar"` + `target_identifier=calendar_id`; on `OutboundResult(suppressed=True)` returns `WriteResult(applied=False, external_id=None, suppressed=True)` per F-3 (HARD); on success returns `WriteResult(applied=True, external_id=response["id"], suppressed=False)`; on `HttpError` classifies + emits `adapter.error@v1` + re-raises. When seams are unset (Phase A — today's supervisor loader behavior) the impl direct-calls `events.insert` without the wrap. `_on_external_change(envelope)` is a Phase A no-op satisfying the Cat-B ABC (push-notification ingress out of scope per parent draft). `_classify_calendar_error` mirrors Gmail's helper via duck-typed `.resp.status` (no `googleapiclient.errors.HttpError` import at module load): 401/403 → `fatal/auth_failed`; 429 → `transient/rate_limited` with `Retry-After` parse; 5xx → `transient/network`; other 4xx → `transient/api_changed`. `_emit_authenticated` / `_emit_error` / `_append` mirror Gmail's helpers.

  - **c4 — pack-internal + integration tests + BUILD_LOG** (this commit):
    - `packs/adapters/calendaring/google-calendar/tests/test_google_calendar_pack_internal.py` (NEW, F-4 unique basename, F-6 NO `__init__.py` in the tests directory, **4 tests**): (1) `test_capabilities_class_attribute_matches_manifest` — the `GoogleCalendarAdapter.capabilities` ClassVar mirrors the manifest's `capabilities` block per memo 05 §3.2 (supervisor reads ClassVar; validator reads manifest; this pack-internal test is the only drift guard); (2) `test_pack_yaml_round_trips_through_parse_adapter_manifest` — Cat-B + `write_capabilities=["create_event"]` + `sharing_model_discriminator="owner_member_id"` + `dedup_strategy={"kind": "external_id"}` + `sensitivity_default="normal"` + `observation_mode_required=True` all round-trip through `parse_adapter_manifest` cleanly; (3) `test_inbound_emit_payload_validates_against_calendar_event_added_v1_schema` — `_emit_calendar_event_added(...)` produces a payload that `CalendarEventAddedV1.model_validate(...)` accepts byte-for-byte (`extra="forbid"` rejects drift; `min_length=1` rejects empties on source/external_event_id/calendar_id/start/end); pack-side guard against accidental schema-shape drift; pins F-2 (MAP DON'T REGISTER) at the pack layer alongside the framework-layer pin in c3; (4) `test_six_validator_checks_return_zero_errors_and_no_advisory` — `validate_adapter_pack(...)` returns zero `error` findings AND no `advisory_pending_retrofit` finding (the latter only attaches to retrofitted v1 packs; this is fresh canonical-build so the advisory is absent by design).
    - `tests/integration/test_adapter_google_calendar_supervisor.py` (NEW, **3 tests**, default-CI sibling): (1) `test_supervisor_loaded_adapter_inherits_external_state_mirror_adapter` — supervisor-loaded class (via file-path importlib — same path the supervisor uses at runtime because `google-calendar`'s hyphen forbids a direct `from packs...` import) is a subclass of `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter` and NOT a subclass of any of the other four ABCs per [PM-34] mutual-exclusion; (2) `test_supervisor_write_capabilities_gate_through_loader` — the [UT-27] gate on the base-class `apply_write` rejects undeclared kinds (`update_event` / `delete_event`) via `WriteCapabilityError` BEFORE the Google Calendar API is touched; pins the contract between the `write_capabilities` ClassVar + base-class gate at the runtime-instance seam; (3) `test_supervisor_inbound_poll_emits_calendar_event_added` — `_poll_once` emits `calendar.event_added@v1` on first observation with a payload that validates against `CalendarEventAddedV1` byte-for-byte, AND re-poll on the same id is a no-op per the inbound dedupe gate (F-3 of c2). F-2 (MAP DON'T REGISTER) pinned at the integration seam alongside the framework + pack-internal pins, defense-in-depth across three layers.
