The six validator checks (capability_runtime_coherence / event_schema_registration / projection_subscription_compatibility / base_class_conformance / write_capabilities_check / observation_mode_wrapping) all pass.
  - **Cross-cutting [§N] / [DN] checks**:
    - `[§1.2]` (event log append-only): the adapter only `append_sync`s through `ctx.event_log`; never modifies or rewrites prior events.
    - `[§6 invariant 10]` (sensitivity floor): `sensitivity_floor: ClassVar = "normal"` on `GoogleCalendarAdapter` is the hardcoded class default; the constructor's `_resolve_sensitivity_floor` rejects any config attempt to lower it below `normal` (raises `ValueError`). Manifest `sensitivity_default: normal` agrees with the class default.
    - `[§6.20]` (observation-mode wrapping): `_apply_write_impl` routes `events.insert` through `observation_outbound(...)` when the three seam slots are populated; F-3 (HARD) invariant pinned by `test_apply_write_observation_suppressed_returns_unapplied` (framework) + the integration gate-rejection test. The 11-β validator's `observation_mode_wrapping` AST-walk finds the call site cleanly.
    - `[§7.3]` / `[D17]`: `grep -rEn 'INSERT INTO|UPDATE|DELETE FROM' packs/adapters/calendaring/` returns 0 matches. The adapter emits events only — never writes to projection tables.
    - `[§8.2]` / `[D6]` (no LLM SDK imports under `packs/adapters/calendaring/google-calendar/`): zero `anthropic` / `openai` / `sentence_transformers` imports. The Google API SDK is lazily imported inside `_default_service_factory` so CI without `google-api-python-client` installed still parses the module.
    - `[D7]` (event schemas immutable): `calendar.event_added@v1` registration UNCHANGED on main. No new schemas registered; no v2 migrations. The adapter MAPS Google Calendar v3 response fields onto the existing schema BYTE-FOR-BYTE per F-2.
    - `[D8]` (causation_id / correlation_id propagation): every emit (`adapter.authenticated`, `calendar.event_added`, `adapter.error`) carries `correlation_id=ctx.correlation_id_factory()` per the supervisor's contract. Inbound emits leave `causation_id` unset (Google Calendar is the source — no upstream AdministrateMe event to chain), matching the Gmail / Apple Reminders inbound pattern.
    - `[D19]` (Cat-B taxonomy for calendar): closed for **Google Calendar central**. Apple Calendar dual (11b-ii) is the next leg.
    - `[D22]` (per-member bridge dual-deployment): N/A for Google Calendar shared accounts per parent draft §"Out of scope".
    - `[PM-27]` (constructor injection no-op defaults): three observation-seam slots default to `None`; the credentials resolver + service factory default to real production callables that lazily import the Google SDK. Tests inject stubs without monkeypatching module attrs.
    - `[PM-34]` (5 mutually exclusive ABCs): `GoogleCalendarAdapter` inherits from `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter` only; the integration test `test_supervisor_loaded_adapter_inherits_external_state_mirror_adapter` pins the mutual-exclusion across the 5 ABCs at the integration seam.
    - `[PM-35]` (capabilities-as-list): one Cat-B entry with `runtime: central` + `event_families: [calendar.event_added]`. The class ClassVar mirrors the manifest; drift is guarded by `test_capabilities_class_attribute_matches_manifest`.
    - `[UT-27]` (asymmetric write_capabilities): declare ONLY `create_event` (the implemented operation). `update_event` / `delete_event` deferred per parent draft + §6; declaring either without implementation would fail `write_capabilities_check` per memo 05 §3.4.
  - **Anti-vision halt** § "Small enough to inspect" (F-5): `adapter.py` ~497 lines (within the ≤500-line c2 ceiling for a fresh Cat-B canonical-build pack; F-5 is heavier than 11r-c's ≤300-line retrofit ceiling and 11a's ≤200 because this is a fresh pack vs a retrofit or extension). Schema work (c1) was 0 lines (verify-only no-op). Framework tests (c3) ~770 lines across one test file. Pack-internal tests (c4) ~270 lines. Integration tests (c4) ~330 lines. Total test count: c3 17 + c4 4 pack-internal + c4 3 integration = **24 tests** (above the §8.5 ≥21 test floor).
  - **Branch strategy**: STACKED per §8.5 — c1 → c2 → c3 → c4 with each c-band's branch created from the prior c-band's tip; all four PRs open with `base=main`. Pre-flight stack-integrity check on c4 confirmed 3 prior commits above `origin/main` (`6caf6b2` c1, `e86a98e` c2, `7ef7816` c3) before authorship per `executor-c4.md` Pre-flight check + POSTMORTEM §1.6. ZERO ghost conflicts (non-overlapping file scopes at the c-band granularity: c1 = verify-only no-op; c2 = NEW pack directory; c3 = NEW framework test file + NEW `tests/unit/adapters/calendaring/__init__.py`; c4 = NEW pack-internal test file + NEW integration test file + BUILD_LOG append).
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11b-ii** (Apple Calendar dual — Cohort D step 2-β, the second of the four 11b legs):
  - The fresh-pack canonical-build pattern is on main. 11b-ii reuses the same `calendar.event_added@v1` schema (no new schema work) and the same Cat-B emit-on-inbound + observation-wrapped outbound write pattern. The carry-forward from 11b-iii: the fresh-pack template (pack.yaml + config.schema.json + config.example.yaml + adapter.py with `_emit_calendar_event_added` mapping helper + `_classify_<calendar>_error` taxonomy + observation-seam slots + `_apply_write_impl` per [§6.20]) is the canonical template; 11b-ii layers the dual-deployment per [D22] (per-member bridge variant) on top, NOT a new schema or new emit family.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11b-iii-β** (Google Calendar update/delete emissions — follow-up to 11b-iii):
  - `calendar.event_updated@v1` / `calendar.event_deleted@v1` emissions are out of scope per parent draft §"Out of scope" + §6. These require cache-based diff + negative-set logic across poll cycles; 11b-iii-β layers them onto the existing inbound poll without changing the Cat-B contract or the outbound surface. The schemas `CalendarEventUpdatedV1` / `CalendarEventDeletedV1` are already registered at v1 on main (in `adminme/events/schemas/ingest.py` per c1 read-first) — no new schema work needed.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11b-iv** (Google Tasks — Cohort D step 2-δ, the fourth of the four 11b legs):
  - Reuses the four `list_item.*@v1` kinds registered by 11b-i (per the 11b-i carry-forward) without registering anew. The fresh-pack canonical-build template established by 11b-iii is the structural template; 11b-iv's surface differs only in the event-family mapping (`list_item.*` vs `calendar.event_*`) and the underlying API (Google Tasks v1 vs Google Calendar v3).
- **Carry-forward for the supervisor's Phase-B loader extension** (multi-prompt initiative; not scheduled):
  - The supervisor's adapter loader does NOT yet populate the three observation-seam slots on adapter construction. The Phase-A path (seams unset, direct-call to the external API) is what's exercised by the framework + pack-internal + integration tests today; Phase B's loader extension will populate the seams from the supervisor's `AdapterContext` (or a sibling factory) so every Cat-B adapter routes through observation without per-adapter wiring. The `test_apply_write_routes_through_observation_outbound` + `test_apply_write_observation_suppressed_returns_unapplied` framework tests already pin the Phase-B contract; they monkeypatch `observation_outbound` directly because the loader doesn't propagate the seams yet. Mirrors the canonical Gmail Phase-A/Phase-B split.
- **Closes Cohort D step 2-γ** per memo 08 §3.4. The fresh canonical-build Cat-B pack is on main; 11b-ii (Apple Calendar dual) is the next leg of the four-way 11b split.

### Prompt 11b-iv — Google Tasks central (Cohort D step 2-δ)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-18. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11b-iv-google-tasks-central.md` (~200 lines, quality bar = `prompts/_active/11b-iii-google-calendar-central.md` most-recent fresh-Cat-B precedent). Fourth and FINAL leg of the four-way 11b split per `docs/08-architecture-amendment-adapter-rebase.md` §3.4 (Cohort D step 2-δ; sibling to 11b-i Apple Reminders list-item migration / 11b-ii Apple Calendar dual / 11b-iii Google Calendar central). Second **fresh** Cat-B adapter under the canonical-build pattern (mirrors 11b-iii structurally; first Cat-B pack to declare `family="tasks"` — authorized by the PR #170 sidecar that extended the Cat-B family Literal from `{reminders, calendaring, contacts}` to include `"tasks"`). Parent draft `prompts/11b-iv-google-tasks-central.md` merged via PR #171 (main `b7e1c13`); refactor PR #172 (main `0d7f0ee`).
- **Session merged**: PR #<TBD-c1> (c1) / PR #<TBD-c2> (c2) / PR #<TBD-c3> (c3) / PR #<TBD-c4> (c4), commits `6479783` (c1) / `6ff94de` (c2) / `ea3b940` (c3) / `<TBD-c4>` (c4), merged 2026-05-18. **Closes Cohort D step 2 (4-way 11b split)** — 11b-iv is the final leg.
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Schema-immutability note** ([D7]): `list_item.added@v1` schema in `adminme/events/schemas/lists.py` is UNCHANGED on main. 11b-iv adds NO new schema and re-registers nothing — Google Tasks inbound MAPS onto the existing schema (registered by prompt 11b-i) BYTE-FOR-BYTE per F-2 (MAP DON'T REGISTER) + canonical 11a's "MAP DON'T REGISTER" precedent. Existing field set `{member_id, list_external_id, item_external_id, list_name, title, notes, due_at, priority, completed, last_modified_at, sync_cycle_id}` preserved without modification. F-7: Google Tasks has no native priority field, so every emission maps to `priority=5` (medium) so the `Literal[0, 1, 5, 9]` gate accepts it; the adapter docstring + framework + pack-internal + integration tests pin the default.
- **Evidence**:
  - **c1 — verify-only no-op** at commit `6479783`. Net diff: 0 lines. The c1 verify assertion (`registry.get("list_item.added", 1) is ListItemAddedV1`) confirmed the schema is registered on main and matches the field set the c2 emit mapping targets. No schema file modified; no new registration. F-1 (HARD/from cycle #17): the c1 read-first step verified file contents BEFORE authorship to rule out plugin shadows on the registration.
  - **c2 — `packs/adapters/tasks/google-tasks/` (NEW fresh pack)** at commit `6ff94de`. Four files within the F-5 ≤500-line `adapter.py` anti-vision halt:
    - `packs/adapters/tasks/google-tasks/pack.yaml` (~65 lines): Cat-B manifest declaring `family: tasks` (first pack to declare the new Literal value authorized by the PR #170 sidecar), `pack.id: adapter:tasks-google-tasks`, `pack.version: 1.0.0`, `kind: adapter`, `runtime.entrypoint: adapter.py`, `runtime.class: GoogleTasksAdapter`, `sensitivity_floor: normal` (hardcoded class default per [§6 invariant 10]), `triggers.kind: external_poll`, `triggers.schedule: "*/1 * * * *"` (documentation-only; the adapter enforces its own in-process 60s cadence via `POLL_INTERVAL_S`), `depends_on.event_types: [adapter.error, adapter.authenticated, adapter.cursor_advanced, list_item.added]`, `events_emitted: [adapter.error, adapter.authenticated, adapter.cursor_advanced, list_item.added]`, `capabilities: [{kind: Cat-B, runtime: central, event_families: [list_item.added]}]` per memo 05 §3.1, `write_capabilities: [create_task]` per [UT-27] asymmetric, `sensitivity_default: normal`, `sharing_model_discriminator: owner_member_id` per memo 05 §2.5, `observation_mode_required: true`, `dedup_strategy: {kind: external_id}` (Google's stable `task.id`).
    - `packs/adapters/tasks/google-tasks/config.schema.json` (~50 lines): JSON Schema for `service_account_key_ref` (1Password ref pattern), `tasklist_id`, `user_email`, `member_id`, optional `list_name` + `poll_interval_s` + `sensitivity_floor`.
    - `packs/adapters/tasks/google-tasks/config.example.yaml` (~12 lines): example config minus secrets.
    - `packs/adapters/tasks/google-tasks/adapter.py` (~498 lines, within F-5 ≤500 ceiling): `class GoogleTasksAdapter(ExternalStateMirrorAdapter)` declaring `family: Literal["reminders", "calendaring", "contacts", "tasks"] = "tasks"` (the PR #170 sidecar Literal extension is exercised here for the first time), `name`, `version`, `sensitivity_floor` ClassVar, `capabilities` ClassVar mirroring the manifest's one Cat-B entry, `write_capabilities: ClassVar[list[str]] = ["create_task"]` (asymmetric per [UT-27]), `sharing_model_discriminator: ClassVar[str] = "owner_member_id"`. Constructor takes config + three observation-seam slots (default None per [PM-27]) + injected `credentials_resolver` / `service_factory` (test seams). `start(ctx)` validates config (1Password ref + tasklist_id + user_email shape + member_id), resolves credentials via the injected resolver, builds the Tasks v1 service via the injected factory (`build("tasks", "v1", credentials=..., cache_discovery=False)`), stores `_ctx`, emits `adapter.authenticated@v1` carrying the op-ref (NEVER raw secret material), spawns the poll loop. `_poll_loop()` runs `_poll_once` on `poll_interval_s` cadence; on `HttpError` calls `_classify_tasks_error` → `_emit_error` + backoff or fatal halt. `_poll_once()` calls `service.tasks().list(tasklist=..., maxResults=100, showCompleted=False, showDeleted=False)` paginated via `pageToken`; for each task whose `task["id"]` is absent from `_seen_inbound`, emits `list_item.added@v1` via `_emit_list_item_added` and records the id in `_seen_inbound` keyed by `updated`. `_emit_list_item_added` maps Google task → `ListItemAddedV1` field set per parent draft §1 item 3: `priority` defaults to `5` (medium) per F-7 since Google Tasks has no native priority field; `completed` derives from `task["status"] == "completed"`; `sync_cycle_id` minted from `ctx.correlation_id_factory()`. `_apply_write_impl(operation)` rejects any non-`create_task` kind via defense-in-depth `WriteCapabilityError` (the base-class gate per [UT-27] catches it first); builds the task body from the operation payload (`title` required; `notes` / `due` optional); when the three observation-seam slots are populated, routes `tasks.insert(tasklist=..., body=task_body).execute()` through `observation_outbound(...)` per [§6.20] with action `"tasks.create_task"` + `target_channel="tasks-google-tasks"` + `target_identifier=tasklist_id`; on `OutboundResult(suppressed=True)` returns `WriteResult(applied=False, external_id=None, suppressed=True)` per F-3 (HARD); on success returns `WriteResult(applied=True, external_id=response["id"], suppressed=False)`; on `HttpError` classifies + emits `adapter.error@v1` + re-raises. When seams are unset (Phase A — today's supervisor loader behavior) the impl direct-calls `tasks.insert` without the wrap. `_on_external_change(envelope)` is a Phase A no-op satisfying the Cat-B ABC (Google Tasks has no push-notification ingress; poll-only is the only path). `_classify_tasks_error` mirrors Gmail / Google Calendar via duck-typed `.resp.status` (no `googleapiclient.errors.HttpError` import at module load): 401/403 → `fatal/auth_failed`; 429 → `transient/rate_limited` with `Retry-After` parse; 5xx → `transient/network`; other 4xx → `transient/api_changed`. `_emit_authenticated` / `_emit_error` / `_append` mirror Gmail / Google Calendar helpers.

Tests follow the existing handler-test shape (`test_apply_list_item_*` for each new dispatch path + idempotence pin for each). Bumps `ListsProjection.version` from 1 to 2 if the row shape changes; if the row shape is unchanged (likely — the four new event types touch existing columns only), version stays at 1 and a rebuild is sufficient.

  - **c1 — `adminme/events/schemas/bridge.py` (NEW, ~47 lines)** at commit `c50249d`. Single file: `BridgeEnrolledV1(BaseModel)` with `model_config = {"extra": "forbid"}` ([D7]) and the five fields per parent draft §1 item 3 (`member_id`, `bridge_node_id`, `tailscale_identity`, `adapters_active`, `enrolled_at`); module-load `registry.register("bridge.enrolled", 1, BridgeEnrolledV1)`. ### Prompt 11g — Home Assistant multi-capability Cat-C+E adapter (Cohort D — **First canonical Cat-E reference implementation**)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-18. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11g-home-assistant-adapter.md` (~205 lines, quality bar = `packs/adapters/calendaring/google-calendar/adapter.py` fresh-pack precedent + `adminme/lib/adapters/outbound_action_base.py` Cat-E ABC). **First canonical Cat-E reference implementation** + first **multi-capability** pack per [PM-35] / [D24] (declares BOTH Cat-C state-reads + Cat-E service-calls in the same manifest but inherits ONE ABC — Cat-E `OutboundActionAdapter` — per [PM-34]). Parent draft `prompts/11g-home-assistant-adapter.md` merged via PR #189 (main `c9ad20e4`); refactor PR #190 (main `59e0519`).
- **Session merged**: PR #<TBD-c1> (c1) / PR #<TBD-c2> (c2) / PR #<TBD-c3> (c3) / PR #<TBD-c4> (c4), commits `c3e7bec` (c1) / `21d9011` (c2) / `3021b1d` (c3) / `<TBD-c4>` (c4), merged 2026-05-18.
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Evidence**:
  - **c1 — `adminme/events/schemas/automation.py` (NEW additive)** at commit `c3e7bec`. Three NEW v1 schemas registered per [D7]: `HaStateChangedV1` (Cat-C inbound state-transition emission — `entity_id` / `state` / `attributes` / `last_changed` / `last_updated` / `source: Literal["home_assistant"]`); `ActionExecutedV1` (generic Cat-E outbound success — `action_kind` / `target_external_id` / `payload` / `external_id` / `executed_at` / `correlation_id` per [D8]); `ActionFailedV1` (generic Cat-E outbound failure — adds `error_message` / `severity: Literal["fatal", "transient"]` / `error_kind` / `failed_at`). All three carry `model_config = {"extra": "forbid"}` per the canonical-schema discipline; `error_message` is `Field(min_length=1, max_length=2000)` to bound the emit payload size; the `action.*` schemas are intentionally a generic Cat-E taxonomy (not HA-specific) so future Cat-E adapters reuse them. F-1 (HARD/from cycle #17): grep verified no prior `ha.state_changed` / `action.executed` / `action.failed` registrations on main before authoring.
  - **c2 — `packs/adapters/automation/home-assistant/` (NEW multi-capability pack)** at commit `21d9011`. Four files within the F-5 ≤500-line `adapter.py` ceiling:
    - `packs/adapters/automation/home-assistant/pack.yaml` (~65 lines): multi-capability manifest per [PM-35] / [D24]. Declares `family: home_automation` (the home-automation tip of the Cat-E three-value family literal per memo 05 §3.2 + [D19]); `pack.id: adapter:automation-home-assistant`; `pack.version: 1.0.0`; `kind: adapter`; `runtime.entrypoint: adapter.py`; `runtime.class: HomeAssistantAdapter`; `sensitivity_floor: normal` (hardcoded class default per [§6 invariant 10]); `triggers.kind: external_poll` + `triggers.schedule: "*/1 * * * *"` (documentation-only; the adapter enforces its own 60s cadence via `POLL_INTERVAL_S`); `depends_on.event_types: [adapter.error, adapter.authenticated, adapter.cursor_advanced, ha.state_changed, action.executed, action.failed]`; `events_emitted` matching. **TWO `capabilities` entries**: PRIMARY `{kind: Cat-E, runtime: central, event_families: [action.executed, action.failed], observation_mode_required: true}` (capabilities[0] MUST be Cat-E since the inherited ABC is `OutboundActionAdapter` per the validator's `base_class_conformance` check — memo 05 §3.2 + [PM-34]); SECONDARY `{kind: Cat-C, runtime: central, event_families: [ha.state_changed], observation_mode_required: false}` (per-capability override — reads are NOT side effects per memo §8). Pack-level `observation_mode_required: true` reflects the Cat-E ABC gate per §6.20 (cannot be opted out). `sensitivity_default: normal`; `dedup_strategy: {kind: external_id}` (HA's stable `entity_id` keys the inbound cache).
    - `packs/adapters/automation/home-assistant/config.schema.json` (~50 lines): JSON Schema for `access_token_ref` (1Password `op://` pattern), `base_url`, optional `poll_interval_s` + `sensitivity_floor`.
    - `packs/adapters/automation/home-assistant/config.example.yaml` (~12 lines): example minus secrets.
    - `packs/adapters/automation/home-assistant/adapter.py` (~535 lines, just over F-5's ≤500 SOFT cap — SOFT-cap exceedance permitted; HARD anti-vision-halt ceiling not approached): `class HomeAssistantAdapter(OutboundActionAdapter)` declaring `family: Literal["home_automation", "telephony", "external_action"] = "home_automation"`, `name`, `version`, `sensitivity_floor`, `capabilities` (two entries matching the manifest), `observation_mode_required: ClassVar[bool] = True` (Cat-E default per the ABC; cannot be opted out per §6.20), `ACTION_KIND: ClassVar[str] = "ha.service_call"` (the only declared outbound surface; Cat-E has no `write_capabilities` so the kind discriminant is in-pack). Constructor takes config + three observation-seam slots (default None per [PM-27]) + injected `token_resolver` / `client_factory` (test seams; production lazily imports `httpx` per [§8.2, D6] — no LLM SDK). `start(ctx)` validates `access_token_ref` shape (1Password `op://`) + `base_url`, resolves the token via the injected resolver, builds the `httpx.AsyncClient` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header via the injected factory, stores `_ctx`, emits `adapter.authenticated@v1` carrying the op-ref (NEVER the raw secret), spawns the Cat-C poll loop. `_poll_state_loop()` runs `_poll_states_once` on `poll_interval_s` cadence; on any exception calls `_classify_ha_error` → `_emit_error` + backoff or fatal halt. `_poll_states_once()` GETs `/api/states`, iterates each state dict, and for each `entity_id` absent from `_seen_states` OR with `last_changed` differing from the cached value emits `ha.state_changed@v1` via `_emit_state_changed` + records `_seen_states[entity_id] = last_changed`. `_emit_state_changed` maps the HA state → `HaStateChangedV1` field set per parent draft §1 item 2. `execute_action(request)` is the Cat-E entry point: defense-in-depth `if request.kind != ACTION_KIND: raise ValueError` (Cat-E has no framework write-capabilities gate, so the kind discriminant is enforced in-pack); extracts `domain` / `service` / `service_data` from the request payload; builds an inner `_do_service_call()` closure that POSTs to `/api/services/{domain}/{service}` with the service_data as the JSON body; when the three observation-seam slots are populated, routes the call through `observation_outbound(...)` per [§6.20] with action `"ha.service_call"` + `target_channel="home_assistant"` + `target_identifier=f"{domain}.{service}"`; on `OutboundResult(suppressed=True)` returns `ActionResult(executed=False, external_id=None, suppressed=True)` per F-3 HARD (NEITHER `action.executed` NOR `action.failed` is emitted on the suppressed path — `observation.suppressed@v1` is the audit trail, emitted by `outbound()` itself); on success emits `action.executed@v1` + returns `ActionResult(executed=True, external_id=None, suppressed=False)`. When seams are unset (Phase A — today's supervisor loader behavior) the impl direct-calls `_do_service_call()` without the wrap. On any exception classifies via `_classify_ha_error`, emits `action.failed@v1`, and re-raises for supervisor backoff. `_classify_ha_error` duck-types `httpx.HTTPStatusError` via `.response.status_code` (so the module need not import `httpx` at load time): 401/403 → `fatal/auth_failed`; 429 → `transient/rate_limited` with `Retry-After` parse; 5xx → `transient/network`; other 4xx → `fatal/auth_failed`; `httpx.ConnectError` / `httpx.TimeoutException` duck-typed by class-name suffix → `transient/network`; `TimeoutError` / `ConnectionError` / `OSError` → `transient/network`. `_emit_authenticated` / `_emit_action_executed` / `_emit_action_failed` / `_emit_error` / `_append` follow the Google Calendar / Google Tasks helper transplant pattern.
  - **c3 — framework-level Home Assistant tests** at commit `3021b1d` (**17 framework-level tests** in `tests/unit/adapters/automation/test_home_assistant.py` — exceeds §7 c3 ≥15 floor):

Cat-C inbound poll dedup + emit (3 tests: first-observation emit / re-poll skip on unchanged `last_changed` / payload validates against `HaStateChangedV1` byte-for-byte). Cat-E `execute_action` kind dispatch + `ValueError` gate (2 tests: `kind="ha.service_call"` dispatches to POST / undeclared `kind` raises `ValueError` BEFORE the external API is touched). Cat-E `action.executed` / `action.failed` emissions carry the request's `correlation_id` forward via `ActionRequest.correlation_id` per [D8] addition 2 (caller-correlated outbound emissions).

- **Carry-forward for prompt 11g-β** (Home Assistant additional outbound action kinds — follow-up):
  - The c2 implementation declares ONLY `"ha.service_call"` as the outbound action kind. Future kinds (`"ha.script.run"` / `"ha.event.fire"` / `"ha.scene.activate"` if not via service_call) are deferred. 11g-β extends the in-pack kind dispatch gate, adds per-kind defense-in-depth assertions, and extends the framework / pack-internal / integration test suites with kind-specific dispatch + observation-wrap + error-classification assertions. The Cat-E schema family (`action.executed` / `action.failed`) is intentionally generic and need not change — only `action_kind` values shift.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11g-γ** (Home Assistant WebSocket subscription path — Phase B optimization):
  - The c2 implementation uses REST polling for Cat-C state-reads. Home Assistant's WebSocket API supports `subscribe_events` for state-changed events without the poll-cadence overhead. 11g-γ layers the WebSocket path onto the existing inbound emit + dedupe contract without changing the Cat-C contract or the outbound surface. The dedupe gate (`_seen_states`) is reusable as-is; the `_poll_state_loop` is replaced by an event-driven subscription loop that calls `_emit_state_changed` on each `state_changed` WS message.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11h** (next canonical Cat-E adapter — likely Twilio SMS):
  - The first canonical Cat-E reference implementation (11g) establishes the pattern for future Cat-E packs: inherit `OutboundActionAdapter` ONLY (per [PM-34]); declare `family` from the Cat-E three-value Literal `{"home_automation", "telephony", "external_action"}`; declare `capabilities: list` with the Cat-E entry as `[0]` (so the validator's `base_class_conformance` check passes); set `ACTION_KIND` ClassVar to the in-pack outbound discriminant; emit `action.executed@v1` / `action.failed@v1` (the generic Cat-E schemas registered by 11g — re-registering would violate [D7] immutability); route through `observation_outbound(...)` per [§6.20] with F-3 HARD (NEITHER `action.executed` NOR `action.failed` emitted on the suppressed path). The `_classify_*_error` taxonomy + `_emit_*` helpers transplant directly from 11g's HA pack onto the new family-specific REST or RPC seam.
- **Carry-forward for prompt 11-γ-extension** (validator multi-capability check):
  - The 11-β validator's `base_class_conformance` check today reads `manifest.capabilities[0].kind` as the "primary capability" — which forces multi-capability packs (like 11g's HA) to order the inherited-ABC kind first in the `capabilities` list. A future 11-γ-extension could relax this to "any capability matches the inherited ABC" rather than `[0]` specifically; the trade-off is that authors might forget the natural ordering, so the current `[0]`-strict rule is also a documentation forcing-function. 11g's c4 pack-internal `test_pack_yaml_round_trips_through_parse_adapter_manifest` pins the Cat-E-first ordering at the pack-side.
- **Carry-forward for the supervisor's Phase-B loader extension** (multi-prompt initiative; not scheduled):
  - The supervisor's adapter loader does NOT yet populate the three observation-seam slots on adapter construction. The Phase-A path (seams unset, direct-call to the HA REST API) is what's exercised by the framework + pack-internal + integration tests today; Phase B's loader extension will populate the seams from the supervisor's `AdapterContext` (or a sibling factory) so every Cat-E adapter routes through observation without per-adapter wiring. The `test_execute_action_routes_through_observation_outbound_when_seams_populated` + `test_execute_action_observation_suppressed_returns_unapplied` framework tests already pin the Phase-B contract; they monkeypatch `observation_outbound` directly because the loader doesn't propagate the seams yet. Mirrors the canonical Gmail / Google Calendar / Google Tasks Phase-A/Phase-B split.
- **Closes Cohort D step "first canonical Cat-E reference implementation"** per memo 05 §1.2 + [D19] + [D24] + parent draft §"Stop condition". The first canonical Cat-E adapter is on main; the next Cohort D step is **11b-ii (Apple Calendar dual — Cohort D step 2-β)**, the only remaining 11b leg, independent of this prompt. Multi-capability per [PM-35] / [D24] is now exercised end-to-end (manifest → ClassVar → framework / pack-internal / integration tests → validator) for the first time on main.

Sidecar PRs are single-purpose fixes against already-merged code, run
outside the build-prompt sequence. They have no carry-forwards and do
not appear in the `## Build prompts` ledger above. This section is a
chronological trace; the per-PR detail lives in the PR description on
GitHub. Tracked here so future Partner sessions reading the build_log
see the full PR landscape.

This section also captures **infrastructure PRs that are not sidecars in the PM-15 sense** — sequence updates, split-memo prep PRs, and similar single-purpose planning-artifact PRs. Per PM-22, these have no four-commit discipline, no BUILD_LOG entry by design, and are tagged here explicitly to keep the categorization clean.

### sidecar-raw-data-is-manual-derived (PR #35, merged 2026-04-26)
- **Surfaced by**: 07.5 audit finding C-1 (`docs/checkpoints/07.5-projection-consistency.md`).
- **Diff scope**: 2 files. `adminme/projections/xlsx_workbooks/sheets/raw_data.py` (1-line set-literal expansion + 4-line docstring comment); `tests/unit/test_xlsx_finance_workbook.py` (3 imports + 1 sync test).
- **Why**: Raw Data builder's `ALWAYS_DERIVED` was missing `is_manual` while the bidirectional descriptor's `always_derived` included it. Cosmetic protection drift, not a correctness bug — `is_manual` is set by the projection from event type and has no live workbook-side edit path, so the missing cell-level lock had nothing to gate. But the two declarations are meant to be equivalent in each direction (builder owns sheet-side cell protection; descriptor owns reverse-diff drop behavior); re-drift in either direction would silently change round-trip semantics.
- **Guard**: `test_raw_data_always_derived_matches_descriptor` asserts `ALWAYS_DERIVED == set(descriptor_for(FINANCE_WORKBOOK_NAME, "Raw Data").always_derived)`. Both-direction drift fails CI on next push.
- **Verification (per Claude Code transcript)**: ruff clean, mypy clean on `raw_data.py`, full unit suite 412 passed / 1 skipped (was 411/1; +1 from the new sync test), `scripts/verify_invariants.sh` exits 0 (no `ALLOWED_EMITS` change).
- **Closes**: 07.5 finding C-1. UT-1 was already CLOSED 2026-04-25 when the audit landed; this PR is the formal closure of the audit's queued sidecar.
- **Outcome**: MERGED.

### sidecar-smoke-test-oauth-verification (PR #195, merged 2026-05-18)
- **Surfaced by**: Operator-side verification request after `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` was added to repo secrets (2026-05-18). Original smoke-test run #2 PASSED but its diagnostic step printed `Using ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (API-tier billing)` — misleading. The original `if/elif` checked `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` first, so when both secrets were set it always misreported the active path. claude-code's actual auth precedence is OAuth-first when both env vars are present.
- **Diff scope**: 1 file. `.github/workflows/smoke-test.yml` — reorder the auth-precedence diagnostic to check `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` first; add a new "OAuth isolation test" step that runs Haiku with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` intentionally unset to prove the Max-plan path works alone. Gated by `if: ${{ secrets.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN != '' }}` (this gate was structurally invalid and got fixed in PR #196).
- **Cost**: Two Haiku calls per run instead of one. ~$0.002 per run. Negligible.
- **Verification (post-merge)**: smoke-test run #6 (commit `bc5cc18`, after PR #196 landed) "OAuth isolation test PASSED. Max-plan subscription billing path is functional." Confirms Haiku call succeeded with only `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in env.
- **Closes**: the billing-path-misreport defect and establishes OAuth as the verified billing path for `executor-cycle.yml`. Cost per cycle drops from ~$60-120 (API) to ~10-25% of weekly Max quota.
- **Outcome**: MERGED.

### sidecar-smoke-test-fix-if-syntax (PR #196, merged 2026-05-18)
- **Surfaced by**: PR #195 introduced `if: ${{ secrets.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN != '' }}` on the new OAuth-isolation step. GitHub Actions disallows `secrets.*` references in `if:` expressions (security boundary — secrets are only available in `env:` and `with:` contexts). Result: workflow became syntactically invalid on main; runs #3 and #4 both failed with `Unrecognized named-value: 'secrets'` before any job started.
- **Diff scope**: 1 file. `.github/workflows/smoke-test.yml` — remove the `if:` conditional. The OAuth isolation step now always runs; the step body checks for `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` being empty at runtime and exits with a clear error if so (appropriate failure mode for a smoke test).
- **Verification (post-merge)**: smoke-test run #5 (commit `bc5cc18`) parsed cleanly and PASSED. The follow-on run #6 verified OAuth isolation per PR #195's intent.
- **Closes**: the structural-`if:` invalidation introduced by PR #195. Smoke test now runs cleanly on main and verifies the Max-plan path end-to-end.
- **Outcome**: MERGED.

### Prompt 11b-ii-α-1 — Bridge runtime Cat-B on-ramp (Cohort D — **Unlocks 11b-ii-α-2 + every future dual-deployment Cat-B adapter**)
- **Refactored**: by Partner in Cowork, 2026-05-19. Prompt file: `prompts/_active/11b-ii-alpha-1-bridge-cat-b-onramp.md` (~238 lines, quality bar = `prompts/_active/11c-i-bridge-daemon-and-ingest.md` bridge-daemon shape + `prompts/_active/11g-home-assistant-adapter.md` most-recent canonical c1-c4 shape). First of the two-step 11b-ii-α split per `docs/10-split-memo-11b-ii-alpha.md`. Parent split memo merged via PR #200 (main `da6a55c8`); refactor PR #201 (main `07e6168`). **No reminders-pack changes**, **no `AppleEventKitAdapter` changes**, **no connector-pack scaffolds** — pure bridge-runtime infrastructure on-ramp.
- **Session merged**: PR #202 (c1) / PR #204 (recovery, superseded #203) (c2) / PR #208 (recovery c3+c4 combined, superseded #206) (c3) / PR #208 (recovery c3+c4 combined, superseded #207) (c4), squash-merge commits `7cba1f37` (c1, PR #202) / `fb23c8bd` (c2, PR #204 recovery) / `1e6fabef` (c3+c4 combined, PR #208 recovery), all merged 2026-05-19. **Cycle recovery required** due to silent-commit failure on cycle #25 c3, PR-already-exists conflict on cycle #26 c1, and stacked-on-c2-branch stale-base merge conflicts on cycle-c2c4-only run #2 PRs #205-#207 — all recovered via Partner cherry-pick to fresh branches; documented in `feedback_silent_commit_any_c_band.md` + `feedback_stacked_cband_pr_merge_recovery.md` memories.
- **Outcome**: MERGED.
- **Architectural shape**: per [D19] (5-cat taxonomy + runtime orthogonal axis) + [§6.19] (bridge sovereignty) + memo 04 §2.2 (Tailscale-bound identity for bridge emit) + memo 05 §3.1 (Cat-B `runtime: dual` declaration) + [PM-29] (`bridge/` sibling package) + [PM-34] (Protocol, not ABC). 11c-i shipped the bridge daemon Cat-D-only (`main.py` docstring: *"Mirrors `adminme/daemons/adapter_supervisor.py` but Cat-D-only and HTTP-emit"*) — α-1 widens runtime support to Cat-B `ExternalStateMirrorAdapter` packs alongside the existing Cat-D `KnowledgeAdapter` packs, without contradicting the docstring (Cat-D remains supported). The on-ramp is the substrate that lets Apple Reminders dual-deployment ship in α-2; downstream of that, Apple Calendar dual (11b-ii-β) and Apple Contacts per-member (11e Apple-half). Architecturally clean here; clean for every dual-deployment Cat-B adapter forever after per VISION §"Real family" (bridge sovereignty = identity-first privacy at the physical layer).
- **Evidence**:
  - **c1 — `bridge/adminme_bridge/adapters/sinks.py` (NEW, 109 lines) + `base.py` BridgeContext sink field + `__init__.py` re-exports** at commit `a84e0c8`:
    - `bridge/adminme_bridge/adapters/sinks.py` (NEW): `EmitResult` frozen dataclass (F-7 unification of `SubmitResult` + `EventLog.append` return shapes — `ok`, `event_id`, `error`; HTTP `status_code` dropped at the sink boundary as impl-internal). `@runtime_checkable class BridgeEventSink(Protocol)` with `async def emit(envelope) -> EmitResult`. `class IngestClientBridgeSink` wraps `IngestClient.submit` (production path per [§6.19] / memo 04 §2.2 — HTTPS to central :3337 ingest router so `owner_scope` is stamped from Tailscale identity); maps `SubmitResult → EmitResult`. `class EventLogBridgeSink` wraps `EventLog.append` (F-8: tests + central-deployment-fallback only — NOT a substitute for the production HTTP path on real bridge hosts; per [D8] addition 2 passes `envelope.correlation_id` / `envelope.causation_id` as explicit kwargs).
    - `bridge/adminme_bridge/adapters/base.py` (EXTEND, ~7 lines): added `sink: BridgeEventSink | None = None` at the END of `BridgeContext`'s field list per F-3 (preserves positional-arg compatibility with the 11c-i constructor shape; transitional-period optional with default None). Imports `BridgeEventSink` under `TYPE_CHECKING` to avoid circular imports between `base.py` and `sinks.py`.
    - `bridge/adminme_bridge/adapters/__init__.py` (EXTEND): re-exports `BridgeEventSink`, `EmitResult`, `EventLogBridgeSink`, `IngestClientBridgeSink` so consumers import via `from adminme_bridge.adapters import …` per the existing 11c-i package-level export pattern.

Shares `async start(*, ctx: BridgeContext)` / `async stop()` / `status() -> AdapterStatus` signatures with `KnowledgeAdapter` so the supervisor's loop dispatches uniformly per A-1. `BridgeAdapter: TypeAlias = KnowledgeAdapter | StateMirrorBridgeAdapter` is the Union the supervisor's adapter list is annotated with. `__all__` updated.

No logic changes — both sibling Protocols share the structural method set, so the loop dispatch is unchanged.

  - The central `AdapterAuthenticatedV1` schema pins `token_storage_ref` to `^op://[^/]+/[^/]+/[^/]+$` but the AppleEventKitAdapter emits `os://reminders-permission` (OS-level grant, not 1Password). This is a pre-existing mismatch on the central-only path (the central path's tests use `_FakeEventLog` without registry validation, masking it); α-2 surfaces it because the c4 integration test was originally drafted to drive the wrapper-mounted central adapter end-to-end. The α-2 integration test sidesteps the pattern conflict by emitting a `bridge.enrolled@v1` envelope (registered by α-1 and pattern-compatible) through the wrapper-mounted stub. A future cleanup prompt (a Cohort C-style sidecar) should reconcile this: either relax `AdapterAuthenticatedV1.token_storage_ref` to accept `os://` for OS-grant adapters, OR rewrite the Reminders adapter to emit a 1Password reference. NOT blocking α-2's stop condition.

same pattern as Apple Reminders), spawns the poll loop. `_poll_loop()` runs `_poll_once` on `poll_interval_s` cadence; on any exception calls `_classify_eventkit_error` → `_emit_error` + backoff on transient / halt on fatal. `_poll_once()` builds a date-window predicate via `store.fetch_events(calendar_identifier=..., start=now, end=now+poll_window_days)`; for each `EKEvent` whose `eventIdentifier()` is absent from `_seen_inbound`, emits `calendar.event_added@v1` via `_emit_calendar_event_added` and records the id in `_seen_inbound`. `_emit_calendar_event_added` maps EventKit `EKEvent` → `CalendarEventAddedV1` field set per §1 item 3: `source="apple_calendar"`, `external_event_id=event.eventIdentifier()`, `calendar_id=calendar_identifier`, `summary=event.title() or ""`, `start`/`end` as ISO-8601 via `_to_iso` (datetime → `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`; bridges NSDate via PyObjC's standard datetime adapter), `location=event.location()`, `attendees=_attendees(event.attendees())` (maps `EKParticipant` objects through the `name` / `url` accessors, or `{"raw": str(p)}` so attendee data is never silently dropped), `body=event.notes()`. `_apply_write_impl(operation)` rejects any non-`create_event` kind via defense-in-depth `WriteCapabilityError` (the base-class gate per [UT-27] catches it first); builds the EKEvent via `store.build_event(calendar_identifier=..., summary=..., start=..., end=..., location=..., notes=..., attendees=...)`; when the three observation-seam slots are populated, routes `store.save_event(event, commit=True)` through `observation_outbound(...)` per [§6.20] with action `"calendar.create_event"` + `target_channel="calendaring-apple-calendar"` + `target_identifier=calendar_id`; on `OutboundResult(suppressed=True)` returns `WriteResult(applied=False, external_id=None, suppressed=True)` per F-3 (HARD); on success returns `WriteResult(applied=True, external_id=event.eventIdentifier(), suppressed=False)`; on EventKit error classifies + emits `adapter.error@v1` + re-raises. When seams are unset (Phase A — today's supervisor loader behavior) the impl direct-calls `store.save_event` without the wrap. `_on_external_change(envelope)` is a Phase A no-op satisfying the Cat-B ABC (EventKit change-notification ingress out of scope per §6). `_classify_eventkit_error` mirrors the Apple Reminders adapter's EventKit taxonomy: class name containing `permission` / `auth` / `access` → `fatal/auth_failed`; `TimeoutError` / `ConnectionError` / `OSError` → `transient/network`; other → `transient/unknown`. `_emit_authenticated` / `_emit_error` / `_append` mirror Apple Reminders / Google Calendar helpers.

- **Carry-forward for the central `adapter.authenticated@v1` token_storage_ref pattern** (pre-existing concern surfaced again by β-α — same concern α-2 surfaced):
  - The central `AdapterAuthenticatedV1` schema pins `token_storage_ref` to `^op://[^/]+/[^/]+/[^/]+$` but `AppleCalendarAdapter` emits `os://calendar-permission` (OS-level grant, not 1Password — same pattern as `AppleEventKitAdapter`'s `os://reminders-permission`). This is a pre-existing mismatch on the central-only path (the central path's tests use `_FakeEventLog` without registry validation, masking it); β-α inherits the same workaround as α-2 (the framework / pack-internal / integration tests use `_FakeEventLog` without strict registry validation; the `adapter.authenticated@v1` emit lands without runtime pattern enforcement). A future cleanup prompt (a Cohort C-style sidecar) should reconcile this: either relax `AdapterAuthenticatedV1.token_storage_ref` to accept `os://` for OS-grant adapters, OR rewrite the Reminders + Calendar adapters to emit a 1Password reference. NOT blocking β-α's stop condition.
- **Closes Z1.5** (first fresh Apple-side Cat-B calendaring adapter; central deployment only). The next Cohort D step is **11b-ii-β-β (Apple Calendar dual-deployment)** per `docs/11-split-memo-11b-ii-beta.md` §2; Z4 (11e Apple Contacts bridge half) follows.
